## Psalms 1:1 (id: 23652)

* *Blessed*: The Hebrew term *’ashrey* appears twenty\-six times in the Psalms. While some translations use “blessed,” a different term (*barak*) is used for blessings ([Psalms 5:12](https://ref.ly/Ps5:12); [45:2](https://ref.ly/Ps45:2); [107:38](https://ref.ly/Ps107:38)).
* The godly are people who trust the Lord and try to live in ways that please him. They avoid living under the influence of sinners.

    Those who do not fear the Lord are “the wicked” ([Psalm 36:1](https://ref.ly/Ps36:1)). They oppose those who love God, do right, and the poor and oppressed (see [10:2–13](https://ref.ly/Ps10:2-Ps10:13); [37:14](https://ref.ly/Ps37:14)). The wicked might seem devoted to God ([50:16](https://ref.ly/Ps50:16)). Sometimes they enjoy success, but it does not last long ([37:35](https://ref.ly/Ps37:35)). They will face sadness and ruin when they must deal with the results of their bad actions ([1:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:4-Ps1:6); [9:16](https://ref.ly/Ps9:16); [32:10](https://ref.ly/Ps32:10)).

* People who turn away from God's path and rebel against him are sinners ([Psalms 25:8](https://ref.ly/Ps25:8); [78:17](https://ref.ly/Ps78:17)). They commit serious crimes like adultery (being unfaithful to their spouse) and murder (compare [Psalm 51](https://ref.ly/Ps51:1-Ps51:19)).
* *sit in the seat of*: This expression describes a person who is controlled by sin (compare [Psalm 1:2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:2); [26:3–7](https://ref.ly/Ps26:3-Ps26:7); [Deuteronomy 6:7](https://ref.ly/Deut6:7)).
* Mockers are people who disrespect and reject God's wisdom. Mockers hate the Lord and insult anyone who tries to correct them ([Proverbs 9:7–8](https://ref.ly/Prov9:7-Prov9:8)). They seek only the “wisdom” that justifies their actions. These bold people have little respect for God, his plans, or his followers (see [Psalms 86:14](https://ref.ly/Ps86:14); [119:51](https://ref.ly/Ps119:51), [69](https://ref.ly/Ps119:69), [78](https://ref.ly/Ps119:78), [85](https://ref.ly/Ps119:85), [122](https://ref.ly/Ps119:122)). These three terms (*wicked, sinners, mockers*) describe all kinds of people who do not live in a way that pleases God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Psalms 1:1–2 (id: 23649)

The Lord brings true happiness to those who turn away from evil and find joy in him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 1:1–6 (id: 23650)

Psalm 1 is a wisdom psalm. It sets the tone for the whole book of Psalms. The psalm clearly contrasts the happiness of godly people with the judgment of the wicked. In the end, the way of wisdom will triumph.

The psalm describes an ideal picture of a godly person, similar to [Psalm 8](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps8:9). This picture shows what the Lord expects from his people. It also points forward to the coming Messiah, God’s chosen king ([Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)). 

The people of Israel did not live up to this ideal. The kings from David’s family also failed to meet it. Even the best kings could not bring about the lasting righteousness described in [Psalms 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6), [2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [and 72](https://ref.ly/Ps72:1-Ps72:20). 

In the New Testament, the apostles use the Psalms to describe the life and work of Jesus the Messiah. Their teaching shows that Jesus alone fulfills the ideal of the godly person described in the Psalms. Only Jesus fully pleased God and secured redemption (rescue from sin), true happiness, and peace.

[Psalm 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6) also introduces a note of hope that continues through the Psalms. It looks forward to the final hallelujah in [Psalm 150](https://ref.ly/Ps150:1-Ps150:6)). At that time, the Lord will remove all evil from his creation and bring blessing and life to his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

## Psalms 1:1–2.12 (id: 23648)

The first two psalms serve as an introduction to the whole book of Psalms (called the Psalter). [Psalm 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6) presents the Lord's guidance in wisdom, while [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12) shows God's rule over a rebellious and sinful world. Together, these psalms invite people to abandon foolishness, which leads to destruction. These psalms invite people to follow God's path instead, which leads to wisdom and salvation. The New Testament confirms that God will govern the nations through Jesus Christ, his chosen representative ([Hebrews 1:5](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5); [Revelation 12:5](https://ref.ly/Rev12:5); [19:15](https://ref.ly/Rev19:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:1-2:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

## Psalms 1:1–8.9 (id: 23651)

[Psalms 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6) and [8](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps8:9) form an **inclusio** (a literary pattern where the beginning and ending act like matching bookends). Together, they describe what God expects from the ideal person. These psalms compare the godly person with the wicked and praise the dignity and worth God gives to the godly (see [Psalms 19](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:14); [33](https://ref.ly/Ps33:1-Ps33:22); [104](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1-Ps104:35); [145](https://ref.ly/Ps145:1-Ps145:21)). 

Jesus the Messiah, introduced in [Psalm 2,](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12) fully embodies this ideal. He is the Lord with complete power and authority, and the perfect example of true humanity. The New Testament applies Psalm 8 to Jesus, showing that God placed everything under his authority ([Hebrews 2:6–8](https://ref.ly/Heb2:6-Heb2:8); see [1 Corinthians 15:27](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`)

## Psalms 1:2 (id: 23653)

* The law of the Lord is called *torah* in Hebrew. It means God’s instruction or teaching for his people (see study note on Deuteronomy 4:44).
* Meditating involves inner reflection, which can be positive ([Psalms 1:2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:2); [63:6](https://ref.ly/Ps63:6); [77:12](https://ref.ly/Ps77:12); [143:5](https://ref.ly/Ps143:5)) or negative (see [Psalms 2:1](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1); [38:12](https://ref.ly/Ps38:12)). The Lord’s word is the object of godly meditation ([119:48](https://ref.ly/Ps119:48)).
* *day and night*: This includes all times, whether walking, standing, sitting, or sleeping (see [Deuteronomy 6:7](https://ref.ly/Deut6:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:2
* **Associated Articles:** Deuteronomy 4:44 (ID: `10537@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 1:5 (id: 23657)

The wicked cannot endure the Lord's judgment. They are already condemned (see [Psalms 5:5](https://ref.ly/Ps5:5); [7:6](https://ref.ly/Ps7:6); [101:7](https://ref.ly/Ps101:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Psalms 1:6 (id: 23658)

* When God takes care of people who obey him and punishes people who do evil, it shows how God gives people what their actions deserve (see [Galatians 6:7–10](https://ref.ly/Gal6:7-Gal6:10)). This is called "retribution." The wicked might appear successful for a short while, but their lives are short and meaningless in the end. God and the people who remain faithful to him will have the final victory (see [Psalms 9:5](https://ref.ly/Ps9:5), [6](https://ref.ly/Ps9:6), [18](https://ref.ly/Ps9:18); [10:16](https://ref.ly/Ps10:16); [37:20](https://ref.ly/Ps37:20); [73:27](https://ref.ly/Ps73:27); [92:9](https://ref.ly/Ps92:9); compare [142:4](https://ref.ly/Ps142:4); [Isaiah 57:1](https://ref.ly/Isa57:1)).
* People who live in a way that pleases God are happy ([Psalms 1:1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1); [5:12](https://ref.ly/Ps5:12); [14:5](https://ref.ly/Ps14:5)). They are happy because God protects and cares for them, listens when they ask for justice, and secures their future (see [121:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps121:3-Ps121:4)).
* A person's path is their life and character.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 1:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:1 (id: 24224)

Their useless paths lead to death ([Psalm 1:4](https://ref.ly/Ps1:4), [6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6)), but sinful human beings join together to oppose the true God and his Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:1-3

## Psalms 2:1–12 (id: 24220)

This royal psalm describes the Lord choosing a king descended from David. It celebrates the mission of all the kings in David's family line. It also introduces the hope of an ideal ruler. This ruler will accomplish the Lord's goal of bringing all nations into submission ([Psalm 2:11](https://ref.ly/Ps2:11)) or to destruction ([2:9](https://ref.ly/Ps2:9)). The New Testament identifies Jesus as this descendant (see [Acts 13:33](https://ref.ly/Acts13:33); [1 Corinthians 15:25–27](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25-1Cor15:27); [Hebrews 1:5](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5); [5:5](https://ref.ly/Heb5:5); [Revelation 19:15](https://ref.ly/Rev19:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`)

## Psalms 2:2 (id: 24225)

The kings hate the Lord's established order in the world, so they want to be independent of him (see [Psalm 36:4](https://ref.ly/Ps36:4)). They plan to dominate the Lord's people ([Psalm 31:13](https://ref.ly/Ps31:13); see [83:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps83:1-Ps83:4)). Their goal is to undermine the Lord's rule by overthrowing his Anointed One (or *his Messiah*). 

The Lord chose the kings of David's family line to establish his kingdom on earth. The New Testament confirms that Jesus, the appointed and anointed Son of God ([Acts 4:26](https://ref.ly/Acts4:26); [13:33](https://ref.ly/Acts13:33); [Hebrews 1:5](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5); [5:5](https://ref.ly/Heb5:5)), fulfills the role in which these earlier kings failed.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:3 (id: 24226)

*break Their chains*: These kings think that recognizing the true God and his chosen ruler will lead to their enslavement ([Isaiah 28:22](https://ref.ly/Isa28:22); [52:2](https://ref.ly/Isa52:2); compare [Lamentations 3:7](https://ref.ly/Lam3:7); [Romans 1:1](https://ref.ly/Rom1:1); [1 Corinthians 7:22](https://ref.ly/1Cor7:22); [Titus 1:1](https://ref.ly/Titus1:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`)

## Psalms 2:4 (id: 24228)

*enthroned in heaven*: The Lord's position in the universe is so secure that he laughs at humans who claim they can oppose his power and authority (see [Psalms 3 7:13](https://ref.ly/Ps37:13); [59:8](https://ref.ly/Ps59:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:4–6 (id: 24227)

The Lord sees the human threat as unimportant. He offered a solution long before they rebelled. He will stop their unrest by appointing a human leader, a descendant of David, to rule the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:6 (id: 24229)

*My King*: The Lord himself appoints and establishes his anointed ruler. The word *anointed* means chosen and set apart by God for a special purpose.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Psalms 2:7 (id: 24231)

*today I have become Your Father*: In the ancient Near East, people often believed in a father\-son relationship between gods and kings. 

In Israel, the Lord adopted kings as his sons. God set David apart as his most excellent and exalted son ([Psalm 89:27](https://ref.ly/Ps89:27)). All descendants of David were the Lord’s representatives or agents. 

Jesus is an eternal member of the Godhead (the one God who exists as Father, Son, and Holy Spirit; [John 1:1](https://ref.ly/John1:1)). He is also a descendant of David ([Acts 13:33](https://ref.ly/Acts13:33); [Hebrews 1:5](https://ref.ly/Heb1:5); [5:5](https://ref.ly/Heb5:5)). Because he is both eternal and from David's family, Jesus uniquely fulfills God’s plan. He is fully divine and truly human. For this reason, he is rightly called the Son of God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:7–9 (id: 24230)

The Lord adopted the king from David's family to conquer rebellious nations by force (see [Psalm 89:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ps89:26-Ps89:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 2:8 (id: 24232)

* In the Old Testament, the relationship between the Lord and Israel’s king can be compared to a suzerain\-vassal relationship. A suzerain was a great king. A vassal was a smaller king who served him.

    In this picture, the Lord is the great King. Israel’s king rules under his authority (see Deuteronomy Book Introduction, “Literary Form”). The Lord invites his anointed king to ask for what he needs (see [Psalm 21:2](https://ref.ly/Ps21:2), [4](https://ref.ly/Ps21:4)).

* All nations will be subject to David's descendant, the Messiah.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`)

## Psalms 2:9 (id: 24233)

The Lord grants his chosen ruler the power to conquer nations as his inheritance ([Isaiah 11:4](https://ref.ly/Isa11:4); [Revelation 2:27](https://ref.ly/Rev2:27); [12:5](https://ref.ly/Rev12:5); [19:15](https://ref.ly/Rev19:15)), ending their rebellion, resistance, and independence.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:9


## Psalms 2:10 (id: 24222)

The Lord is pleased when people submit to him and seek his protection. Wise people accept the Lord's correction and want to change.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`)

## Psalms 2:11–12 (id: 24223)

* *rejoice*: Leaders should accept the Son as their true and only King.
* *Kiss the Son*: Kissing was an expression of submission ([Hosea 13:2](https://ref.ly/Hos13:2)).
* The Lord gives joy to those who fear him. *To fear the Lord* means to honor him, respect him, and obey him ([Psalm 1:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:2)). The Psalms also say that the Lord protects those who trust him ([18:30](https://ref.ly/Ps18:30); [34:22](https://ref.ly/Ps34:22); [119:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps119:1-Ps119:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 2:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 3:1 (id: 24357)

This difficult time in David’s life (see [2 Samuel 15–18](https://ref.ly/2Sam15:1-2Sam18:33)) helps readers understand the promises of [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12) better. The Lord gave victory to his chosen king ([Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)), but God controlled how and when this victory happened.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 3:1–2 (id: 24354)

* A *psalmist* is the writer or speaker of a psalm. The psalmist here faces many enemies. Those enemies speak boldly and without respect ([Psalm 4:6](https://ref.ly/Ps4:6); [12:4](https://ref.ly/Ps12:4); [40:15](https://ref.ly/Ps40:15)). They question whether the Lord can really save him ([10:11](https://ref.ly/Ps10:11); [22:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps22:7-Ps22:8); [71:10](https://ref.ly/Ps71:10)). Their words trouble the psalmist because it seems the Lord will not answer his prayer.
* These enemies hold great power (compare [Psalm 2:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:3)). Unlike [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), this psalm does not resolve the crisis.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 3:1–8 (id: 24356)

This psalm expresses sorrow that the hopes from [Psalms 1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps2:12) have not been fulfilled. Even when surrounded by enemies, godly people should not doubt the Lord's just actions. Instead, they can trust in God's goodness and expect God to rescue them.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`)

## Psalms 3:1–7.17 (id: 24355)

This group of psalms moves from an orderly world to a troubled one. [Psalms 1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps2:12) describe stability. The righteous person is blessed, and the Lord’s anointed king rules with authority.

In the psalms that follow, the tone changes. The Lord’s anointed cries out for help. He faces enemies, strong opposition, and evil plans.

It even seems at times that the Lord is far away. The psalmist feels confusion and distress instead of confidence and calm.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:1-7:17

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:3-4

## Psalms 3:5 (id: 24360)

By focusing on the Lord ([Psalm 3:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps3:3-Ps3:4)) during his crisis, the psalmist can rest, confident in his safety (see study note on Psalm 4:8). The Lord cares for his servant. God protects him and keeps his mind and heart free from fear ([Psalms 37:17](https://ref.ly/Ps37:17); [54:4](https://ref.ly/Ps54:4); [119:116](https://ref.ly/Ps119:116); [145:14](https://ref.ly/Ps145:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:5
* **Associated Articles:** Psalms 4:8 (ID: `24501@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 3:6 (id: 24361)

The psalmist feels peaceful and confident because the Lord protects and cares for him. This shields him from evil and gives him a new understanding of reality ([Psalm 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4); [27:1](https://ref.ly/Ps27:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Ps27:3); [46:1](https://ref.ly/Ps46:1); [56:4](https://ref.ly/Ps56:4), [11](https://ref.ly/Ps56:11); [91:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps91:5-Ps91:6); [118:6](https://ref.ly/Ps118:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 3:7–8 (id: 24362)

* The psalmist does not seek revenge. Instead, he waits for the Lord to take vengeance.
* *Arise*: The psalmist asks the Lord to act now.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 3:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 4:1 (id: 24496)

Waiting for the Lord's answer helps you grow in patience and strengthen your faith ([Psalm 38:15](https://ref.ly/Ps38:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 4:1–8 (id: 24495)

This personal lament includes: 

* a prayer for rescue ([Psalm 4:1](https://ref.ly/Ps4:1))
* a rebuke of the enemy ([Psalm 4:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps4:2-Ps4:3))
* advice to the faithful ([Psalm 4:4–5](https://ref.ly/Ps4:4-Ps4:5))
* a statement of confidence ([Psalm 4:6–8](https://ref.ly/Ps4:6-Ps4:8))

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Psalms 4:2 (id: 24497)

* The opponents of those who follow God rely on false beliefs, deceit, and idol worship.
* *my honor* (or *my glory*): The psalmist's honor, or possibly the Lord's, is being challenged.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Psalms 4:4 (id: 24498)

* Feeling angry is not a sin, but letting anger control you can lead to sin (see [Psalm 37:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps37:7-Ps37:8); compare [Ephesians 4:26–27](https://ref.ly/Eph4:26-Eph4:27), [31–32](https://ref.ly/Eph4:31-Eph4:32)).

* Instead of acting on their emotions, godly people think about their circumstances overnight. They might be disturbed as they sleep, but silence opens their hearts to trust in the Lord ([Psalm 4:5](https://ref.ly/Ps4:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Psalms 4:5 (id: 24499)

*Offer the sacrifices of the righteous*: Redirecting emotions toward the Lord prevents a godly person from doing too much self\-reflection ([Psalms 40:6–8](https://ref.ly/Ps40:6-Ps40:8); [51:17](https://ref.ly/Ps51:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 4:6 (id: 24500)

*the light of Your face*: When the psalmist speaks about God’s face, he is asking for God’s favor and peace ([Numbers 6:25–26](https://ref.ly/Num6:25-Num6:26)). God’s favor can change a situation from darkness to light (see [Psalm 27:1](https://ref.ly/Ps27:1)). 

Confident faith protects against anger, resentment, jealousy, and a desire for wealth as the highest good. When God’s favor is enough, other desires lose their power.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 4:8 (id: 24501)

Peace comes from God's blessing and protection (see [Psalm 4:6](https://ref.ly/Ps4:6) and [37:11](https://ref.ly/Ps37:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 4:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 5:1–2 (id: 24588)

The phrase *give ear to my words* appears in several prayers as a call for God's attention during difficult times ([Psalms 39:12](https://ref.ly/Ps39:12); [84:8](https://ref.ly/Ps84:8); [143:1](https://ref.ly/Ps143:1); compare [Psalms 17:1](https://ref.ly/Ps17:1); [54:2](https://ref.ly/Ps54:2); [130:2](https://ref.ly/Ps130:2)). The needy ask their God, “Are you listening?”

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 5:1–12 (id: 24587)

This morning prayer ([Psalm 5:3](https://ref.ly/Ps5:3)) shows strong trust in the Lord's help and protection. The psalmist asks for God's response and waits for it ([Psalm 5:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps5:1-Ps5:3)). 

He describes the plans of the wicked in detail but stays focused on God. The psalmist knows the wicked cannot be with God ([Psalm 5:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps5:4-Ps5:6), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps5:9); see [1:5](https://ref.ly/Ps1:5)). 

He keeps returning to the Lord ([Psalm 5:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps5:7-Ps5:8), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps5:10); see [5:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps5:1-Ps5:3)), and then he prays for the godly community ([5:10–12](https://ref.ly/Ps5:10-Ps5:12); see [3:8](https://ref.ly/Ps3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Large Shield (ID: `realia:LargeShield`)

## Psalms 5:3 (id: 24592)

* The psalmist starts and ends each day with prayer, looking for new signs of God's faithfulness and goodness ([Psalm 143:8](https://ref.ly/Ps143:8)).
* *wait in expectation*: The psalmist does not demand God's grace but hopes for it (see [Micah 7:7](https://ref.ly/Mic7:7); [Habakkuk 2:1](https://ref.ly/Hab2:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 5:5 (id: 24593)

* *The boastful*: Mockers (see study note on Psalm 1:1), the wicked ([Psalm 73:3](https://ref.ly/Ps73:3); [75:4](https://ref.ly/Ps75:4)), and the arrogant ([10:2](https://ref.ly/Ps10:2)) use their words to distort the truth and challenge the faith of the godly.
* The Lord allows only the godly into his presence (see [Psalm 1:5](https://ref.ly/Ps1:5)). His holiness and justice do not tolerate oppression or evil actions.
* *You hate*: The Lord rejects proud sinners (see also [Psalm 11:5](https://ref.ly/Ps11:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:5
* **Associated Articles:** Psalms 1:1 (ID: `23652@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`)

## Psalms 5:7 (id: 24594)

* God promises his loving devotion (his covenant goodness and faithfulness) to his people (see [Psalm 25:6](https://ref.ly/Ps25:6); [69:16](https://ref.ly/Ps69:16); [Lamentations 3:32](https://ref.ly/Lam3:32)).
* *Your house*: The righteous desire to enter God's presence with awe and wonder (see [Hebrews 12:28](https://ref.ly/Heb12:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 5:8 (id: 24595)

* God guides those who are obedient, submissive, and patient as they wait for him to lead, provide, and protect them ([Psalm 27:11](https://ref.ly/Ps27:11); [43:3](https://ref.ly/Ps43:3); [143:10](https://ref.ly/Ps143:10)). He does not guide those who expect God simply to approve their own decisions.
* Following the right path of wisdom leads to the Lord ([Psalms 47:11](https://ref.ly/Ps27:11); [61:2](https://ref.ly/Ps61:2); [139:24](https://ref.ly/Ps139:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 5:9–10 (id: 24596)

The psalmist again describes the terrible acts of the wicked, who cannot stand in God’s presence.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 5:10 (id: 24589)

* The psalmist prays for the justice and retribution that God promised against the wicked ([Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6)). The wicked are those who have rebelled against the Lord.
* The wicked set traps to harm ([Psalm 38:12](https://ref.ly/Ps38:12)) or trick others ([140:4–5](https://ref.ly/Ps140:4-Ps140:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 5:11 (id: 24590)

* A blessing rests on all who take refuge in God (see [Psalm 18:30](https://ref.ly/Ps18:30)).
* Joy and praise go well together. The godly rejoice when they see evidence of God’s rescue. They sing joyful praises as a new song (see [Psalm 33:3](https://ref.ly/Ps33:3)).

    When the Lord brings peace to the world, defeats evil, and rewards good people, they will have lasting joy ([Psalms 9:2–4](https://ref.ly/Ps9:2-Ps9:4); [53:6](https://ref.ly/Ps53:6); [90:14–15](https://ref.ly/Ps90:14-Ps90:15); [118:24](https://ref.ly/Ps118:24)).

* The children of God, who find refuge in him, love his name. To love his name is to love his very character.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`)

## Psalms 5:12 (id: 24591)

* The psalmist cares not only for himself but for all the godly people.
* A shield*,* here an image of God’s protection, deflects spears and arrows from the one holding it.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 5:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Large Shield (ID: `realia:LargeShield`)

## Psalms 6:1 (id: 24685)

*do not rebuke me … or discipline me*: The psalmist does not clearly connect God's discipline with sin here (compare [Psalm 39:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps39:8-Ps39:11)), but it might be implied.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 6:1–3 (id: 24684)

The psalmist asks for God’s favor and restoration at a difficult time.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`)

## Psalms 6:1–10 (id: 24683)

The occasion for this lament might have been sickness or a mental or spiritual depression from which the psalmist sought healing.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Psalms 6:2–3 (id: 24686)

The psalmist became physically weak while waiting for God's answer to his prayer ([Psalm 6:6](https://ref.ly/Ps6:6); see [77:3](https://ref.ly/Ps77:3); [119:81](https://ref.ly/Ps119:81); [142:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps142:1-Ps142:3); see also [Psalm 101](https://ref.ly/Ps101:1-Ps101:8)). Fasting (abstaining from eating food) might have contributed to his suffering, but it is not mentioned.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`)

## Psalms 6:4–5 (id: 24687)

The experience was so painful that the psalmist might as well have been dead*,* or perhaps he feared for his life.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 6:5 (id: 24688)

*from Sheol?*: In the Old Testament, Sheol is the place of the dead. It is not necessarily associated with punishment.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Psalms 6:6–7 (id: 24689)

* The psalmist feels deeply exhausted due to his spiritual distance from the Lord ([Psalms 22:1](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1); [31:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ps31:9-Ps31:10); [102:5](https://ref.ly/Ps102:5)).
* When eyes *grow dim,* it means the body is failing (see [Psalms 13:3](https://ref.ly/Ps13:3); [31:9](https://ref.ly/Ps31:9); [38:10](https://ref.ly/Ps38:10); [Matthew 6:22](https://ref.ly/Matt6:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Psalms 6:8–10 (id: 24690)

In a closing note of triumph, the psalmist reasserts his confidence in the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 6:8-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`)

## Psalms 7:1 (id: 24811)

*Pursuers*, a common term for *enemies*, actively look for people in order to harm or destroy them ([Psalm 31:15](https://ref.ly/Ps31:15); [56:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps56:1-Ps56:2); [71:11](https://ref.ly/Ps71:11); [143:3](https://ref.ly/Ps143:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:1–17 (id: 24805)

This psalm includes the following: 

* A lament ([Psalm 7:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps7:1-Ps7:2)): The psalmist cries out to God for help and protection from enemies.
* An oath of innocence ([Psalm 7:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps7:3-Ps7:5)): The psalmist declares that he has done no wrong and maintains his integrity.
* A call for God's justice ([Psalm 7:6–9](https://ref.ly/Ps7:6-Ps7:9)): He asks God to act against evildoers and defend the righteous.
* An affirmation of God’s just kingship ([Psalm 7:10–16](https://ref.ly/Ps7:10-Ps7:16)): The psalmist praises God as a fair judge who rules the world rightly.
* A concluding hymn of praise ([Psalm 7:17](https://ref.ly/Ps7:17)): The psalmist ends with a song of thanksgiving, honoring God’s righteousness and deliverance.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:1-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Pit (ID: `realia:Pit`)

## Psalms 7:title (id: 24820)

An unknown person from the tribe of Benjamin remained hostile to David.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Pit (ID: `realia:Pit`)

## Psalms 7:2 (id: 24812)

Like a lion, a wicked enemy relentlessly and violently pursues its prey ([Psalms 10:9](https://ref.ly/Ps10:9); [17:12](https://ref.ly/Ps17:12); [22:13](https://ref.ly/Ps22:13), [21](https://ref.ly/Ps22:21); [35:17](https://ref.ly/Ps35:17); [57:4](https://ref.ly/Ps57:4)), but God promises victory ([91:13](https://ref.ly/Ps91:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`)

## Psalms 7:3 (id: 24814)

The psalmist does not claim to be without sin, but he knows that he does not deserve the cruel attacks of his opponents (see [35:7](https://ref.ly/Ps35:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:3–5 (id: 24813)

The psalmist declares his innocence, opening himself up to examination ([Psalm 7:9](https://ref.ly/Ps7:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:5 (id: 24815)

* *trample*: Compare [Psalm 44:5](https://ref.ly/Ps44:5); [60:12](https://ref.ly/Ps60:12); [91:13](https://ref.ly/Ps91:13); [108:13](https://ref.ly/Ps108:13).
* *to the ground … in the dust*: Disgrace and shame can lead to death and the grave ([Psalm 143:3](https://ref.ly/Ps143:3); see [13:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps13:3-Ps13:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:5


## Psalms 7:6 (id: 24817)

God’s judgment brings justice*,* despite the chaos created by enemies (see [7:11](https://ref.ly/Ps7:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:6–9 (id: 24816)

With this appeal to God for justice in the world, the psalmist also submits to God’s examination.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:6-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:8 (id: 24818)

* The judgment is against the defendants (the peoples) and in favor of the righteous plaintiff (the psalmist; see [Psalm 43:1](https://ref.ly/Ps43:1)).
* The psalmist, though not free from all sin, is a person of true integrity (see [Psalm 15:2](https://ref.ly/Ps15:2); [25:21](https://ref.ly/Ps25:21); [41:12](https://ref.ly/Ps41:12); [78:72](https://ref.ly/Ps78:72); compare [Job 1:1](https://ref.ly/Job1:1), [8](https://ref.ly/Job1:8); [9:20](https://ref.ly/Job9:20), [22](https://ref.ly/Job9:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:9 (id: 24819)

God knows the thoughts and intentions of every person. He does not see only outward actions. He examines what is hidden in the mind and the heart (see [Psalms 11:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps11:3-Ps11:4); [26:2](https://ref.ly/Ps26:2); [139:1](https://ref.ly/Ps139:1),[23](https://ref.ly/Ps139:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:10–13 (id: 24806)

The psalmist moves from his own situation ([Psalm 7:1–9](https://ref.ly/Ps7:1-Ps7:9)) to speak for all the godly who seek redemption ([7:10–16](https://ref.ly/Ps7:10-Ps7:16)). He protects and vindicates his people. When his followers cry out for help, God hears them and acts to rescue them.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`)

## Psalms 7:11 (id: 24807)

The righteous judge cannot be bought or bribed. He does not abuse his power ([Psalm 9:4](https://ref.ly/Ps9:4); compare [82:1–8](https://ref.ly/Ps82:1-Ps82:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 7:12–13 (id: 24808)

*sword … bow … deadly weapons … arrows with fire*: As the Divine Warrior, God attacks to defend himself and his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`)

## Psalms 7:14 (id: 24809)

Evil starts with small words or actions and leads to worse deeds over time (see [Isaiah 59:4–5](https://ref.ly/Isa59:4-Isa59:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`)

## Psalms 7:17 (id: 24810)

* *I will thank … sing praise*: The laments in [Psalms 3–7](https://ref.ly/Ps3:1-Ps7:17) usually shift from lament to praise, as does the entire book of Psalms.
* *Most High*: This is an ancient title for God (see [Genesis 14:19](https://ref.ly/Gen14:19)).
* God is just and reliable, so his order prevails in this world.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 7:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 8:1 (id: 24934)

* The LORD (Hebrew *Yahweh*) is the covenant God of Israel ([Exodus 3:15](https://ref.ly/Exod3:15)). This name emphasizes God’s faithfulness and relationship with his people.

    He is also Lord (Hebrew *’adonay*), which emphasizes his authority. As Lord, he is the king over the earth and all creation.

* The word translated as *majestic* (and sometimes *mighty*) can describe:

    + kings ([Psalm 136:18](https://ref.ly/Ps136:18)),
        + heroes ([Psalm 16:3](https://ref.ly/Ps16:3)),
        + mountains ([Psalm 76:4](https://ref.ly/Ps76:4)), or
        + waves breaking on the seashore ([Psalm 93:4](https://ref.ly/Ps93:4)).
        While all these things are awe\-inspiring and mighty, they are less impressive than the Lord.

* A person’s name represents their reputation.
* The earth and the heavens reveal the Lord’s splendor. All creation worships and praises the Lord ([Psalm 66:4](https://ref.ly/Ps66:4); [113:3](https://ref.ly/Ps113:3); [145:21](https://ref.ly/Ps145:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 8:1–9 (id: 24933)

[Psalm 8](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps8:9) begins the second part of Book One of the Psalms ([Psalms 8–14](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps14:7)). The psalmist praises God’s creation and the dignity of the ideal human (see [Psalm 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6)). Instead of the world of enemies, wicked people, and unfair justice shown in [Psalms 3–7](https://ref.ly/Ps3:1-Ps7:17), this psalm presents the world as God first made it. Someone who is tired (both physically and emotionally) can imagine a place of dignity in God’s world.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 8:2 (id: 24935)

* *silence*: The loud noise of the adversaries contrasts with the praise of God. In the end, the praise of God will overwhelm the sound of evil ([Psalms 31:18](https://ref.ly/Ps31:18); [63:11](https://ref.ly/Ps63:11); [101:5](https://ref.ly/Ps101:5); [143:12](https://ref.ly/Ps143:12)).
* Those who oppose God are filled with vengeful anger ([Psalm 2:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:3); [44:16](https://ref.ly/Ps44:16); compare [149:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps149:6-Ps149:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:2


## Psalms 8:3 (id: 24937)

Many nations near Israel worshiped the sun, moon, and stars as gods. They thought these objects controlled life and events.

The psalmist reminds us that, while the sun, moon, and stars are great and beautiful, they are only created by God.

Because these things are his creation, they point to God’s power and majesty. They give a reason to praise the Almighty, who alone is worthy of worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:3


## Psalms 8:3–5 (id: 24936)

God’s glory is vast and awe\-inspiring. At first, it might seem that people are too small or unimportant to matter to him.

Yet God has given humans honor and dignity. He has crowned them with glory and set them in charge of creation. 

This shows that every person has value. Humans are called to care for and rule over the world responsibly, reflecting God’s glory in their lives.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 8:4 (id: 24938)

*son of man* (literally *son of Adam*): This means "a human being." Through the influence of [Daniel 7:13–14](https://ref.ly/Dan7:13-Dan7:14), *son of man* became a title with divine meaning in the New Testament. See [Hebrews 2:6–8](https://ref.ly/Heb2:6-Heb2:8), where this passage is quoted.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Psalms 8:5 (id: 24939)

*You made him a little lower than the angels*: Humans bear God’s image. The Lord has given humans dignity and tasked them to rule ([Genesis 1:26–27](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26-Gen1:27)). 

[Hebrews 2:6–8](https://ref.ly/Heb2:6-Heb2:8) applies these words to Jesus Christ, the ideal human who fully realized God’s purposes.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 8:6 (id: 24940)

*made him ruler of the works of Your hands*: The psalmist is still referring to humans in general. 

Paul applies these words to Jesus as the perfect man who has made the created order, and even death ([1 Corinthians 15:25–27](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25-1Cor15:27)), subject to God and his glory. 

The New Testament connects these themes of creation and salvation with Jesus Christ ([John 1:1–2](https://ref.ly/John1:1-John1:2), [14](https://ref.ly/John1:14); [Colossians 1:16](https://ref.ly/Col1:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:6


## Psalms 8:7–8 (id: 24941)

Wild animals, including birds and fish, remind us of God's creation of the animal world in [Genesis 1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31). Contrary to beliefs among Israel’s neighbors in the ancient Near East, animals are not sacred.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 8:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 9:1 (id: 25058)

* The verb translated as *give* *thanks* is also translated as *thank* in [Psalm 7:17\.](https://ref.ly/Ps7:17) This shows a strong connection between [Psalms 7](https://ref.ly/Ps7:1-Ps7:17) and [9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:1-Ps9:20) (see [7:17](https://ref.ly/Ps7:17); [8:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps8:2)).

    The phrase *with all my heart* shows the psalmist’s deep devotion and commitment ([Psalms 86:12](https://ref.ly/Ps86:12); [111:1](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1); [119:10](https://ref.ly/Ps119:10); [138:1](https://ref.ly/Ps138:1)).

* *I will recount*: These stories teach the next generation ([Psalms 78:4](https://ref.ly/Ps78:4); [145:4](https://ref.ly/Ps145:4)). If forgotten, a generation might easily turn away from the Lord ([Psalms 78:11](https://ref.ly/Ps78:11), [32](https://ref.ly/Ps78:32)).
* *Your wonders*: These include the Lord’s judgments on the enemies of his people ([Psalm 9:4](https://ref.ly/Ps9:4)) and his care for the oppressed ([9:9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:9)). The wonders in creation, redemption, and revelation show his character and power ([Psalms 89:5](https://ref.ly/Ps89:5); [111:4](https://ref.ly/Ps111:4); [119:18](https://ref.ly/Ps119:18), [27](https://ref.ly/Ps119:27)). His powerful acts inspire public praise ([Psalm 9:1](https://ref.ly/Ps9:1); [26:7](https://ref.ly/Ps26:7); [75:1](https://ref.ly/Ps75:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:title (id: 25066)

However, that tune (or song) is no longer known. The same is true for the tunes mentioned in [Psalms 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps45:17), [56–60](https://ref.ly/Ps56:1-Ps60:12), [69](https://ref.ly/Ps69:1-Ps69:36), [75](https://ref.ly/Ps75:1-Ps75:10), [and 80](https://ref.ly/Ps80:1-Ps80:19).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 9:1–10.18 (id: 25050)

This hymn celebrates the Lord's rule and triumph over evil. It may also respond to the laments in [Psalms 3–7](https://ref.ly/Ps3:1-Ps7:17). 

Some believe that [Psalms 9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:1-Ps9:20) and [10](https://ref.ly/Ps10:1-Ps10:18) should be read together as one psalm in two parts. This is because [Psalm 9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:1-Ps9:20) has a title, while [Psalm 10](https://ref.ly/Ps10:1-Ps10:18) does not. 

Despite some missing or inverted letters, the two psalms form an acrostic of the Hebrew alphabet. This means that each line or section begins with a successive letter of the Hebrew alphabet.

Other alphabetic acrostics include [Psalms 25](https://ref.ly/Ps25:1-Ps25:22), [34](https://ref.ly/Ps34:1-Ps34:22), [37](https://ref.ly/Ps37:1-Ps37:40), [111–112](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1-Ps112:10), [119](https://ref.ly/Ps119:1-Ps119:176), and [145](https://ref.ly/Ps145:1-Ps145:21). 

The ancient Greek and Latin translations treat Psalms 9 and 10 as one psalm.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:1-10:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 9:2 (id: 25059)

*rejoice … will sing praise*: The psalmist often connects joy with praises, as one leads to the other ([Psalm 5:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ps5:11-Ps5:12); [32:11](https://ref.ly/Ps32:11); [67:4](https://ref.ly/Ps67:4); [90:14](https://ref.ly/Ps90:14); [92:4](https://ref.ly/Ps92:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:3 (id: 25061)

*retreat … stumble*: The Lord overpowers even powerful enemies (see [Psalm 27:2](https://ref.ly/Ps27:2)). Israel also experienced judgment when it resisted the Lord ([107:12](https://ref.ly/Ps107:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:3


## Psalms 9:3–6 (id: 25060)

The psalmist praises God's judgment of the wicked. This shows the Lord's promise to establish his kingdom on earth is trustworthy ([Psalms 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6); [2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:3-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Psalms 9:4 (id: 25062)

The throne represents the Lord’s royal authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:4


## Psalms 9:5 (id: 25063)

God created the heavens and the earth with a word ([Genesis 1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31)), and he can end nations with a word (see [Psalm 2:5](https://ref.ly/Ps2:5)). 

Those who do evil will not endure forever. God promises that their power and influence will come to an end ([Psalm 69:28](https://ref.ly/Ps69:28); [Psalm 109:13](https://ref.ly/Ps109:13); [Revelation 21:4](https://ref.ly/Rev21:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:5


## Psalms 9:7–10 (id: 25064)

The godly do not rely on human governments, which can be weak or corrupt. They understand that earthly rulers are limited and sometimes unfair.

Instead, they put their hope in God’s eternal rule. God governs with perfect justice and fairness (see [Psalms 96–100](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps100:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`)

## Psalms 9:9 (id: 25065)

* The Lord offers shelter in dangerous situations and protection from powerful and oppressive people. The psalms often describe the LORD as:

    + a fortress ([Psalm 18:2](https://ref.ly/Ps18:2); [31:2](https://ref.ly/Ps31:2)),
        + a rock ([Psalm 18:2](https://ref.ly/Ps18:2); [42:9](https://ref.ly/Ps42:9)),
        + strength ([Psalm 18:1](https://ref.ly/Ps18:1)), and
        + a strong tower ([Psalm 144:2](https://ref.ly/Ps144:2)).
        These images appear frequently in royal prayers (see [Psalm 9:9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:9); [18:30](https://ref.ly/Ps18:30)).

* Even oppressed, afflicted, and marginalized people ([Psalm 10:17](https://ref.ly/Ps10:17); [74:21](https://ref.ly/Ps74:21)) can receive divine protection.
* Times of trouble ([Psalm 10:1](https://ref.ly/Ps10:1); [20:1](https://ref.ly/Ps20:1)) contrast with moments when the Lord openly shows favor to the afflicted ([69:13](https://ref.ly/Ps69:13); [102:13](https://ref.ly/Ps102:13); see [Psalm 107:6](https://ref.ly/Ps107:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:10 (id: 25051)

At times, a person may feel separated from the Lord. The psalms describe moments of spiritual alienation or distress ([Psalms 22:1](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1); [42:9](https://ref.ly/Ps42:9); [119:8](https://ref.ly/Ps119:8))

Even in these times, the psalms affirm God’s faithfulness. The Lord does not abandon his people ([16:10](https://ref.ly/Ps16:10); [37:25](https://ref.ly/Ps37:25), [28](https://ref.ly/Ps37:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:11 (id: 25053)

The psalm writers often connect the idea that God dwells (or *sits,* or *is enthroned*) with a specific place, such as: 

* heaven ([Psalm 2:4](https://ref.ly/Ps2:4); see [47:9](https://ref.ly/Ps47:9)),
* Jerusalem,
* "between the cherubim"([Psalms 80:1](https://ref.ly/Ps80:1); [99:1](https://ref.ly/Ps99:1)),
* "on high" ([Psalm 113:5](https://ref.ly/Ps113:5)), or
* "at my right hand" ([Psalm 110:1](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1)).

Since the Lord rules the world, everyone must know about his unforgettable deeds of salvation (see [Psalm 9:1](https://ref.ly/Ps9:1); [64:9](https://ref.ly/Ps64:9); [66:16](https://ref.ly/Ps66:16); [105:1](https://ref.ly/Ps105:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:11–12 (id: 25052)

Understanding that the Lord judges some wicked people ([Psalm 9:3–6](https://ref.ly/Ps9:3-Ps9:6)) and promises to judge all evil ([9:7–10](https://ref.ly/Ps9:7-Ps9:10)) inspires praise. The psalmist invites the godly community to join him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:12 (id: 25054)

The Lord saves his servants and punishes those who have harmed them ([Psalm 18:46](https://ref.ly/Ps18:46)). He defends his servants by fairly judging his enemies on earth ([Psalm 58:11](https://ref.ly/Ps58:11)). Those who follow God do not seek revenge. Instead, they wait for the Lord to defend them ([Psalm 94:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps94:1-Ps94:2); [Deuteronomy 32:35](https://ref.ly/Deut32:35); [Romans 12:19](https://ref.ly/Rom12:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`)

## Psalms 9:13–14 (id: 25055)

* The psalmist returns from a vision of the glorious future to the reality of facing his enemies ([Psalm 9:3](https://ref.ly/Ps9:3)).
* The *gates* are a strong image of the powerful grip of death (see [Psalm 13:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps13:3-Ps13:4)).
* The *gates of Daughter Zion* (that is, Jerusalem) provide an intentional contrast to the *gates of death*.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 9:18 (id: 25056)

Hope requires waiting for the Lord’s rescue or justice ([Psalms 25:3](https://ref.ly/Ps25:3), [5](https://ref.ly/Ps25:5), [21](https://ref.ly/Ps25:21)). The godly experience hope, rest, and peace as they wait for the Lord (see [Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6); [27:14](https://ref.ly/Ps27:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Psalms 9:19–20 (id: 25057)

* The psalmist concludes his lament by asking God to act on his behalf. He prays for the Lord’s intervention against those who do wrong. This includes a request for divine vengeance (God’s just punishment of evildoers). The psalmist trusts that only God can bring true justice.
* *but men*: People who gain power and honor can become oppressive and assume glory for themselves (see [Psalm 8:4](https://ref.ly/Ps8:4); [10:18](https://ref.ly/Ps10:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 9:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:1 (id: 23666)

The opening questions set the mood. The Lord does not seem to hear the prayers of his people ([Psalm 22:1](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1), [19](https://ref.ly/Ps22:19); see [2 Kings 4:27](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:27); [Isaiah 1:15](https://ref.ly/Isa1:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:1–18 (id: 23659)

The lament in [Psalm 9:13–20](https://ref.ly/Ps9:13-Ps9:20) continues in [Psalm 10](https://ref.ly/Ps10:1-Ps10:18) (see study note on Psalms 9–10). However, the mood shifts from confident statements to anguished questions. The psalmist prays for rescue, trusting that the Lord, as a just king, take care of the needy.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:1-18
* **Associated Articles:** Psalms 9:1-10.18 (ID: `25050@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`)

## Psalms 10:2 (id: 23668)

The wicked openly defy God with their actions and words ([Psalm 17:10](https://ref.ly/Ps17:10); [31:18](https://ref.ly/Ps31:18); [59:12](https://ref.ly/Ps59:12); [73:8](https://ref.ly/Ps73:8); [123:4](https://ref.ly/Ps123:4)). Although the Lord will deal with them ([Psalm 31:23](https://ref.ly/Ps31:23)), they trouble the righteous (see [Psalm 5:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps5:4-Ps5:6)) before their condemnation and judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`)

## Psalms 10:2–11 (id: 23667)

Wicked and oppressive people might succeed, causing harmful effects. Evil can be as dramatic as the psalmist portrays it here, or it could be more subtle.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:2-11

## Psalms 10:3 (id: 23669)

Unlike those who have evil desires*,* the godly wait for justice ([Psalm 10:17](https://ref.ly/Ps10:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:4 (id: 23670)

*there is no God*: See [Psalms 10:11](https://ref.ly/Ps10:11); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Ps14:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`); A Man (ID: `person:GenericMale`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:6–7 (id: 23671)

Even though they act wrongly ([Psalms 5:9](https://ref.ly/Ps5:9); [28:3](https://ref.ly/Ps28:3); [Romans 3:14](https://ref.ly/Rom3:14)), the wicked still hope for a future free of trouble like the righteous will enjoy.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`)

## Psalms 10:8 (id: 23672)

The wicked rely on violence ([Psalms 11:5](https://ref.ly/Ps11:5); [37:32](https://ref.ly/Ps37:32)) and deception ([Proverbs 12:6](https://ref.ly/Prov12:6); [23:27–28](https://ref.ly/Prov23:27-Prov23:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:8

## Psalms 10:12–15 (id: 23660)

With confidence that the wicked will be held responsible for their deeds and that the Lord will care for the helpless, the psalmist prays intensely for rescue from evil.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:12-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`)

## Psalms 10:14 (id: 23661)

The Lord cares for those who suffer. He will notice and punish those who do evil (see [Psalm 72:14](https://ref.ly/Ps72:14); [116:15](https://ref.ly/Ps116:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:14


## Psalms 10:15 (id: 23662)

* The psalmist prays and calls for a strong curse (see “Prayers for Vengeance” Theme Note).
* *Arms* represent the ability to wage war ([Psalms 18:34](https://ref.ly/Ps18:34); [44:3](https://ref.ly/Ps44:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:15
* **Associated Articles:** Prayers for Vengeance (ID: `500274@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)


## Psalms 10:16 (id: 23663)

As the true and just king*,* God will bring the wicked to an end, as he promises ([Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:17–18 (id: 23664)

The psalmist expresses his confidence in the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:17-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 10:18 (id: 23665)

People often misuse their power and enslave the very people God wants them to serve (see [Psalms 9:19–20](https://ref.ly/Ps9:19-Ps9:20); [Luke 22:25–26](https://ref.ly/Luke22:25-Luke22:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 10:18


## Psalms 11:1 (id: 23826)

* God offers protection (*refuge;* see [Psalms 5:11](https://ref.ly/Ps5:11); [9:9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:9); [25:20](https://ref.ly/Ps25:20)) when justice fails.
* The mountains offer another place of refuge (see [Psalm 121:1](https://ref.ly/Ps121:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 11:1
