* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Psalms 71:1–24 (id: 24823)

This untitled lament by an aging believer is more a confession of confidence and hope than a cry of abandonment by God. The psalmist experienced God's power and protection in his youth ([Psalm 71:5–8](https://ref.ly/Ps71:5-Ps71:8)) and now prays for rescue from enemies who pursue him in his old age ([Psalm 71:9–13](https://ref.ly/Ps71:9-Ps71:13)). 

Even in danger, he continues to speak of God's past faithfulness and looks forward with hope to sharing with the next generation that God is faithful and righteous.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:1-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Psalms 71:5–6 (id: 24831)

The psalmist has trusted God throughout his life (see [Psalm 22:9](https://ref.ly/Ps22:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 71:9 (id: 24833)

The poet prays for continued fellowship with God (see [Psalm 9:10](https://ref.ly/Ps9:10); [51:11](https://ref.ly/Ps51:11)). In his weakness, the poet needs the Lord's strength even more (see [Psalm 143:7](https://ref.ly/Ps143:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Psalms 71:9–18 (id: 24832)

The aging believer turns to the Lord for hope. As questions from his adversaries get under his skin and his own strength fails, he asks God to answer his prayers. He does not seek answers to prayer for himself but so that he can tell the story to the next generation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:9-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 71:10 (id: 24825)

The psalmist's enemies plan and scheme together ([Psalms 2:2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:2); [21:11](https://ref.ly/Ps21:11); [31:13](https://ref.ly/Ps31:13); [35:4](https://ref.ly/Ps35:4); [63:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ps63:9-Ps63:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:10


## Psalms 71:14 (id: 24826)

The psalmist compares his goodness to his enemies' evil. He focuses on the Lord, even in difficult times (see [Psalms 9:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps9:17-Ps9:18); [27:13–14](https://ref.ly/Ps27:13-Ps27:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:14


## Psalms 71:19–21 (id: 24827)

God’s righteous character provides encouragement and strength in the midst of suffering. The wounded psalmist confesses faith in God’s ability to transform his misery and weakness into abundant life.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:19-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 71:20 (id: 24828)

*You will bring me back up*: The psalmist hopes for the renewal of an abundant earthly life. This later became grounds for belief in the resurrection of the dead.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:20


## Psalms 71:21 (id: 24829)

Knowing God provides comfort also assures the poet of God's presence, care, and goodness ([Psalms 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4), [6](https://ref.ly/Ps23:6); [86:17](https://ref.ly/Ps86:17)). God's comfort increases the psalmist's sense of being alive (see [Psalm 119:50](https://ref.ly/Ps119:50)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:21


## Psalms 71:22–24 (id: 24830)

As he awaits God’s rescue, the psalmist prepares himself to declare with instruments and voice that God is faithful and righteous.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 71:22-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`)

## Psalms 72:1 (id: 24845)

*justice … righteousness*: In the ancient Near East, kings were expected to represent justice. For Israel's kings, the standard was higher because they followed the Lord's example.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 72:1–7 (id: 24835)

Justice brings balance to nature, leading to harmony, prosperity, and fertility.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 72:1–20 (id: 24834)

This royal psalm concludes Book Two, with [Psalm 72:18–20](https://ref.ly/Ps72:18-Ps72:20) serving as an epilogue for the entire book. The psalmist considers the future of David's royal line and Zion (see [Psalms 46](https://ref.ly/Ps46:1-Ps46:11), [48](https://ref.ly/Ps48:1-Ps48:14)). He prays that Israel's kings will be good and successful, spreading the Lord's blessing to all people worldwide. The exceptional righteousness and rule desired in this prayer hint at the future arrival of Jesus, the Son of David.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:1-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Seba (ID: `place:Seba`); Tarshish (ID: `place:Tarshish`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); David (ID: `person:David`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jesse (ID: `person:Jesse`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Hyena (ID: `fauna:Hyena`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 72:3 (id: 24847)

Justice makes the world fruitful, abundant, harmonious, and good (see [Isaiah 32:16–17](https://ref.ly/Isa32:16-Isa32:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Psalms 72:4 (id: 24848)

A just ruler will defend the poor against injustice, rescue them from violence, and bring their oppressors to account for their wrongdoing.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:4


## Psalms 72:5 (id: 24849)

*May they fear him*: In Hebrew, this phrase appears to be a response to the enforcement of justice. The Greek version reads *May they endure,* which makes this a prayer for long life ([Psalm 21:4](https://ref.ly/Ps21:4); [72:15](https://ref.ly/Ps72:15), [17](https://ref.ly/Ps72:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 72:6–7 (id: 24850)

* The administration of justice is refreshing like spring rain, allowing the godly to thrive (see [Isaiah 45:8](https://ref.ly/Isa45:8)) like flowers.
* *until the moon is no more*: This phrase means forever (see [Psalm 89:36–37](https://ref.ly/Ps89:36-Ps89:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Psalms 72:8–9 (id: 24852)

* *from sea to sea*: This phrase represents the entire world.
* Solomon's kingdom stretched from the Euphrates River in the east to the Philistine territory in the west ([1 Kings 4:21](https://ref.ly/1Kgs4:21); see [Psalms 80:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps80:8-Ps80:11); [89:19–25](https://ref.ly/Ps89:19-Ps89:25)), where the Mediterranean Sea appeared to be at the ends of the earth (see [2:8](https://ref.ly/Ps2:8)).

    Everyone will bow before this king, including desert nomads ([Psalm 72:9](https://ref.ly/Ps72:9)), his enemies, and all the world's kings (see [Micah 7:17](https://ref.ly/Mic7:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Hyena (ID: `fauna:Hyena`)

## Psalms 72:8–11 (id: 24851)

The anointed king will rule the entire world. Even enemy nations will obey him. In the ancient Near East, giving gifts to the king showed his success and reputation (see [1 Kings 10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs10:1-1Kgs10:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tarshish (ID: `place:Tarshish`); Seba (ID: `place:Seba`); Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Hyena (ID: `fauna:Hyena`)

## Psalms 72:10 (id: 24836)

* The western kings of Tarshish came from a major colony in the western Mediterranean. The eastern kings of Sheba came from southern Arabia, home of the queen of Sheba ([1 Kings 10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs10:1-1Kgs10:29)).
* Seba was likely south of Egypt, possibly on the western shore of the Red Sea across from Sheba.
* The gifts given to the king were a tribute and a sign of submission.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tarshish (ID: `place:Tarshish`); Seba (ID: `place:Seba`); Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Psalms 72:11 (id: 24837)

All kings and nations must submit to the messianic king (God's chosen leader). The hope in [Psalm 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Ps2:10-Ps2:11) will come true (see [96:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:3); [1 Corinthians 15:25](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25)) when other leaders and peoples bow and serve this king.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`)

## Psalms 72:12 (id: 24839)

To *deliver the needy* means to bring an end to the rule of their oppressors.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:12


## Psalms 72:12–14 (id: 24838)

The anointed king will help those in need. He is both a judge and a father to the poor ([Psalms 10:14](https://ref.ly/Ps10:14), [17](https://ref.ly/Ps10:17); [68:5](https://ref.ly/Ps68:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:12-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`)

## Psalms 72:13 (id: 24840)

Like a father, the king feels pity for those in need (see [Malachi 3:17](https://ref.ly/Mal3:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:13


## Psalms 72:15 (id: 24842)

The people will bless him, fulfilling God's promise to Abraham ([Genesis 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3); see [Psalm 133:3](https://ref.ly/Ps133:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 72:15–17 (id: 24841)

The just kingdom will be long\-lasting and universal. The summary combines the themes of abundance of crops, longevity, tribute of the nations, and the prayer of the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:15-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sheba (ID: `place:Sheba.2`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 72:16 (id: 24843)

*the forests of Lebanon*: Lebanon was famous for its wood (see study note on 2 Chronicles 2:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:16
* **Associated Articles:** 2 Chronicles 2:8 (ID: `14067@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 72:18–19 (id: 24844)

This doxology (song of praise) ends Book Two of the Psalms.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Psalms 72:20 (id: 24846)

This parenthetical note ends Book Two, which consists mostly of prayers of David. Books Three to Five were probably compiled after Book Two; they include additional psalms of David ([Psalms 86](https://ref.ly/Ps86:1-Ps86:17), [101](https://ref.ly/Ps101:1-Ps101:8), [103](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:22), [108–110](https://ref.ly/Ps108:1-Ps110:7), [122](https://ref.ly/Ps122:1-Ps122:9), [124](https://ref.ly/Ps124:1-Ps124:8), [131](https://ref.ly/Ps131:1-Ps131:3), [133](https://ref.ly/Ps133:1-Ps133:3), [138–145](https://ref.ly/Ps138:1-Ps145:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 72:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Jesse (ID: `person:Jesse`)

## Psalms 73:1 (id: 24859)

People with pure hearts (people who are sincere and devoted to God) are renewed by God’s Spirit ([Psalm 51:10](https://ref.ly/Ps51:10)). They choose to live in a godly way, turn away from sin, and enjoy God’s presence (see [Psalm 24:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps24:3-Ps24:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph.2`)

## Psalms 73:1–28 (id: 24853)

This wisdom psalm looks at the injustice of the prosperity of the wicked. The psalmist states that God is good to the godly, but his own experience is different ([Psalm 73:2–12](https://ref.ly/Ps73:2-Ps73:12)). 

Almost overwhelmed by doubt ([Psalm 73:13–16](https://ref.ly/Ps73:13-Ps73:16)), the psalmist meets the Lord in the sanctuary. This meeting gives him a broader view and renews his trust in God ([Psalm 73:17–26](https://ref.ly/Ps73:17-Ps73:26)). His troubling doubts lead to a stronger desire for truth. He knows he can trust God and that God will save him ([Psalm 73:27–28](https://ref.ly/Ps73:27-Ps73:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:1-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 73:title (id: 24867)

Asaph was a Levitical singer appointed by David ([1 Chronicles 6:39](https://ref.ly/1Chr6:39)). His descendants were singers and instrumentalists ([1 Chronicles 15:16–17](https://ref.ly/1Chr15:16-1Chr15:17)). Many of the Psalms of Asaph ([Psalms 50](https://ref.ly/Ps50:1-Ps50:23), [73–83](https://ref.ly/Ps73:1-Ps83:18)) were likely written by Asaph’s descendants later in Israel’s history (for example, [Psalm 74](https://ref.ly/Ps74:1-Ps74:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 73:1–75.10 (id: 24854)

The seeming success of the wicked ([Psalm 73](https://ref.ly/Ps73:1-Ps73:28)) and God's apparent rejection of his people ([Psalm 74](https://ref.ly/Ps74:1-Ps74:23)) raise questions about his justice. God rules the entire world and decides when to judge ([Psalm 75](https://ref.ly/Ps75:1-Ps75:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:1-75:10

## Psalms 73:2–3 (id: 24860)

* The poet thinks it is unfair that wicked people prosper (see [Psalm 72:7](https://ref.ly/Ps72:7)) and feels upset about it.
* The psalmist almost strayed from the path of wisdom and godliness (see [Psalm 17:5](https://ref.ly/Ps17:5); [37:31](https://ref.ly/Ps37:31)), but God prevented him from completely losing his way ([38:16](https://ref.ly/Ps38:16); [66:9](https://ref.ly/Ps66:9); [94:18](https://ref.ly/Ps94:18); [121:3](https://ref.ly/Ps121:3)).
* *when I saw the prosperity of the wicked*: Compare [Psalm 72:3](https://ref.ly/Ps72:3), [7](https://ref.ly/Ps72:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Zealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Psalms 73:4–12 (id: 24864)

The psalmist gives an exaggerated picture of the happiness of the wicked (compare [Psalm 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:4-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 73:6 (id: 24865)

Proud people choose pride and cruelty over wisdom ([Proverbs 1:9](https://ref.ly/Prov1:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:6


## Psalms 73:9 (id: 24866)

The proud boast, claiming that they own everything and are not subject to anyone (see [Psalm 2:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:9


## Psalms 73:13–14 (id: 24855)

* These rhetorical questions expect a discouraging “yes” as an answer. The psalmist wonders if godliness has become a meaningless ritual (see [Malachi 3:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mal3:14-Mal3:15)).
* *my heart*: This refers to his whole being.
* The psalmist’s pain comes from discipline and rebuke ([Psalm 39:11](https://ref.ly/Ps39:11); [Proverbs 1:23](https://ref.ly/Prov1:23), [25](https://ref.ly/Prov1:25); [12:1](https://ref.ly/Prov12:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`)

## Psalms 73:13–17 (id: 24856)

When the psalmist sees the effect of his discouragement and doubt, he turns to the Lord and gains new understanding.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:13-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Innocence (ID: `keyterm:Innocence`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 73:15–16 (id: 24857)

If the psalmist had spoken about his doubts regarding the value of living a godly life, he might have discouraged God's people and given God's enemies a chance to speak against God (compare [John 13:20](https://ref.ly/John13:20)). Instead, the psalmist keeps the problem to himself and thinks about it.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:15-16


## Psalms 73:17–20 (id: 24858)

* During this period, God's sanctuary was the temple in Jerusalem. In God's presence, the psalmist receives special insight (compare [Psalm 18:6](https://ref.ly/Ps18:6)).
* God deals with the wicked over the long term. He waits patiently and gives them time to repent ([Ezekiel 33:11](https://ref.ly/Ezek33:11); [2 Peter 3:9](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:9)). Those who do not repent will get what they deserve.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:17-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 73:21–22 (id: 24861)

The psalmist’s feelings about the prosperity of the wicked had given him a bitter spirit.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:21-22


## Psalms 73:24 (id: 24862)

The Lord leads the godly through their troubles and changes their misery into splendor.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Psalms 73:25–26 (id: 24863)

Nothing in heaven or on earth is better than being near God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 73:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

In the aftermath of Jerusalem’s fall, it seemed that God had forgotten his special covenant relationship with his people.

## Psalms 74:15 (id: 24872)

*ever\-flowing rivers*: God gave water in the desert to his people (see [Psalm 68:8–9](https://ref.ly/Ps68:8-Ps68:9); [Exodus 17:6](https://ref.ly/Exod17:6)). He dried up the Red Sea ([Exodus 14:21](https://ref.ly/Exod14:21)) and the Jordan River ([Joshua 3:17](https://ref.ly/Josh3:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 74:15

## Psalms 75:1–10 (id: 24877)

This hymn of thanksgiving includes two direct messages from God that assure us of his justice ([Psalm 75:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps75:2-Ps75:3), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps75:10)). The Lord warns arrogant people who defy him, promising to humble them by force ([Psalm 75:4–8](https://ref.ly/Ps75:4-Ps75:8)). Together, the community and the psalmist give thanks for God's character ([Psalm 75:1](https://ref.ly/Ps75:1), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps75:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 75:1-10

## Psalms 77:1–20 (id: 24890)

The psalmist laments that God has turned away from him, even though he feels innocent. He reflects on his situation ([Psalm 77:3](https://ref.ly/Ps77:3), [6](https://ref.ly/Ps77:6), [12](https://ref.ly/Ps77:12)) and finds hope (see [25:5](https://ref.ly/Ps25:5)) in remembering God's past actions ([77:11](https://ref.ly/Ps77:11)).

## Psalms 77:3 (id: 24896)

* *I remembered You, O God*: God's people often face hard times. Remembering God's powerful actions at the beginning of their relationship assures them of God's commitment.
* Feeling overwhelmed*,* the psalmist is unable to deal with the crisis ([Psalms 142:3](https://ref.ly/Ps142:3); [143:4](https://ref.ly/Ps143:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 77:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 77:4–6 (id: 24897)

As the psalmist meditates at night, he feels so disturbed that he cannot sleep or speak. He has pleasant memories of the past, but he cannot look beyond the present moment into the future.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 77:4-6

## Psalms 78:2 (id: 24909)

* The psalmist tells Israel’s story ([Psalm 78:5–72](https://ref.ly/Ps78:5-Ps78:72)) as a parable to teach wisdom and insight.
* Jesus quoted this verse to explain why he taught in parables ([Matthew 13:35](https://ref.ly/Matt13:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Proverb (ID: `keyterm:Saying`)

## Psalms 78:3–4 (id: 24910)

This story of what God has done should be told from generation to generation (see [Deuteronomy 6:20–25](https://ref.ly/Deut6:20-Deut6:25); [Proverbs 4:1–4](https://ref.ly/Prov4:1-Prov4:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 78:5–6 (id: 24917)

The telling of the story should motivate God’s people to obey what he commanded ([Deuteronomy 6:4–9](https://ref.ly/Deut6:4-Deut6:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Psalms 78:7–8 (id: 24922)

God holds each generation responsible for its own actions. They should maintain faith and hope (see [Psalm 9:18](https://ref.ly/Ps9:18); [27:14](https://ref.ly/Ps27:14)) and avoid being stubborn, rebellious, and unfaithful (see [Deuteronomy 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Deut9:6-Deut9:7); [10:16](https://ref.ly/Deut10:16); [30:6](https://ref.ly/Deut30:6); [31:27](https://ref.ly/Deut31:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`)

## Psalms 78:9–11 (id: 24924)

Ephraim was the second son of Joseph. He received a special blessing from Jacob ([Genesis 48:15–20](https://ref.ly/Gen48:15-Gen48:20); [49:22–26](https://ref.ly/Gen49:22-Gen49:26); [Deuteronomy 33:13–17](https://ref.ly/Deut33:13-Deut33:17)). His descendants inherited land with fertile valleys and important roads ([Judges 8:2](https://ref.ly/Judg8:2)). However, the people of Ephraim did not follow God's covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`)

## Psalms 78:12 (id: 24903)

The *wonders* are the ten plagues against Egypt that God performed during Israel’s captivity ([Exodus 7–12](https://ref.ly/Exod7:1-Exod12:51)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`)

## Psalms 78:12–16 (id: 24902)

The focus shifts from Ephraim ([Psalm 78:9–11](https://ref.ly/Ps78:9-Ps78:11)) to all of Israel. The psalmist thinks about God's power in Egypt (see [Psalm 81:4–7](https://ref.ly/Ps81:4-Ps81:7)) and in the wilderness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:12-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`)

## Psalms 78:13 (id: 24904)

God *split the sea* when Israel crossed through the Red Sea (see [Exodus 14–15](https://ref.ly/Exod14:1-Exod15:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:13


## Psalms 78:14 (id: 24905)

God used *a cloud* and *a light of fire* to guide and protect Israel in the wilderness ([Exodus 13:21–22](https://ref.ly/Exod13:21-Exod13:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 78:14

Israel rebelled in the wilderness despite God’s abundant provision.

During the time of the judges, the people built shrines to false gods and worshiped idols ([Psalm 78:58](https://ref.ly/Ps78:58); see also [Judges 2:11](https://ref.ly/Judg2:11); [3:7](https://ref.ly/Judg3:7), [17–18](https://ref.ly/Judg3:17-Judg3:18)).

The poet laments the defilement of the temple and the success of the enemy.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 79:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph.2`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`)

## Psalms 79:1–13 (id: 24925)

This lament describes the fall of Jerusalem in 586 BC. Israel’s early history of rebellion, described in [Psalm 78](https://ref.ly/Ps78:1-Ps78:72), angered God. As a result, Zion was destroyed. The temple was defiled, and the bodies of the dead lay across the land. 

The psalmist then prays for forgiveness and rescue ([Psalm 79:8–9](https://ref.ly/Ps79:8-Ps79:9)) and asks God to repay the nation's enemies ([Psalm 79:12–13](https://ref.ly/Ps79:12-Ps79:13)). Other psalms lamenting the exile include [Psalms 42–44](https://ref.ly/Ps42:1-Ps44:26), [102](https://ref.ly/Ps102:1-Ps102:28), [107](https://ref.ly/Ps107:1-Ps107:43), [126](https://ref.ly/Ps126:1-Ps126:6), and [137](https://ref.ly/Ps137:1-Ps137:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 79:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 79:5–8 (id: 24929)

The intensity of God’s judgment surprises the people; they cry out in despair for compassion, forgiveness, and relief. Verse [6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps6:1-Ps7:17) are nearly identical to [Jeremiah 10:25](https://ref.ly/Jer10:25).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 79:5-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 79:6 (id: 24930)

The repentant among God’s people ask him to vent his anger not on them but on their wicked and unrepentant enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 79:6


## Psalms 79:8 (id: 24931)

The people’s many sins in the past helped lead to the exile ([2 Kings 17:7–23](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:7-2Kgs17:23); [24:3–4](https://ref.ly/2Kgs24:3-2Kgs24:4)). However, each person also suffered because of his or her own sin ([Jeremiah 31:30](https://ref.ly/Jer31:30); [Ezekiel 18:20](https://ref.ly/Ezek18:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 79:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Psalms 79:12 (id: 24927)

* The exile resulted in national suffering and in individual searching for the Lord.
* *Pay back*: See [Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6); [94:2](https://ref.ly/Ps94:2); [Jeremiah 32:18](https://ref.ly/Jer32:18).

* Israel's neighbors included Edom, Moab, and Ammon (see [Psalm 137:7](https://ref.ly/Ps137:7); [2 Kings 24:2](https://ref.ly/2Kgs24:2); [Obadiah 1](https://ref.ly/Obad1:1-Obad1:21)).

## Psalms 80:1–19 (id: 24942)

This psalm of lament may come from the remnant (the small group that survived) of the northern kingdom after it fell in 722 BC. The people call on God as their Shepherd and as the God of Hosts (a title that describes God as the ruler over the armies of heaven). They hope that he will return to them and restore them.

They remember the Exodus and their special relationship with the Lord. The psalm ends with a promise of renewed commitment to him ([Psalm 80:18](https://ref.ly/Ps80:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 80:1-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Tribe of Benjamin (ID: `group:Benjamin`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Boar (ID: `fauna:Boar`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

However, the people forgot the source of their prosperity and chose to do evil ([Isaiah 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7); [Hosea 10:1](https://ref.ly/Hos10:1)).

## Psalms 81:2 (id: 24961)

* An Israelite tambourine was a hand drum with a round frame and skin cover. People used it as a percussion instrument during celebrations, often to accompany dancing ([Psalms 68:25](https://ref.ly/Ps68:25); [149:3](https://ref.ly/Ps149:3); [150:4](https://ref.ly/Ps150:4)).
* The *sweet\-sounding harp and lyre*, both stringed instruments, are the most frequently mentioned instruments in the psalms (for example, [Psalms 33:2](https://ref.ly/Ps33:2); [57:8](https://ref.ly/Ps57:8); [71:22](https://ref.ly/Ps71:22); [150:3](https://ref.ly/Ps150:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 81:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`)

## Psalms 81:3 (id: 24962)

* The *ram’s horn* gathered armies and announced special holy days ([Psalms 47:5](https://ref.ly/Ps47:5); [98:6](https://ref.ly/Ps98:6)).
* *New Moon*: The first day of each lunar month was a festival.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 81:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** New Moon Festival (ID: `keyterm:NewMoonFestival`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`)

## Psalms 82:2 (id: 24969)

Because justice defines God’s rule ([Psalm 89:4](https://ref.ly/Ps89:4)), he does not support the wicked. The psalmist is confused by this situation and cries out to God to bring justice ([Psalm 82:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps82:3-Ps82:4), [8](https://ref.ly/Ps82:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 82:2


## Psalms 82:5 (id: 24970)

Unjust oppressors cannot bring or restore order. They wander about in darkness*,* unmoved by circumstances, even when the whole world order falls apart around them (compare [Psalm 11:3](https://ref.ly/Ps11:3)).

The gods (see study notes on Psalm 29:1–2; 58:1), like mere humans, cannot escape the judgment common to human rulers. Jesus quoted from this passage in [John 10:34](https://ref.ly/John10:34).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 82:6-7
* **Associated Articles:** Psalms 29:1-2 (ID: `24347@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Psalms 58:1 (ID: `24670@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Psalms 83:1–18 (id: 24973)

This community lament asks God to rescue his people and give them victory. The short and direct poetry suggests a time of great trouble. A strong group of nations plans to destroy Israel ([Psalm 83:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps83:1-Ps83:4)) and joins together against God ([83:5–8](https://ref.ly/Ps83:5-Ps83:8)). 

The psalmist remembers what happened to other enemies who attacked Israel in the past ([Psalm 83:9–12](https://ref.ly/Ps83:9-Ps83:12)). He prays that the same thing will happen to these enemies ([83:13–16](https://ref.ly/Ps83:13-Ps83:16)), so that they will recognize God’s power and give him honor ([83:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps83:17-Ps83:18)). 

It is not clear whether this psalm refers to one specific historical event or whether it gathers together several nations as a poetic picture of Israel’s enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:1-18

## Psalms 83:5–8 (id: 24978)

*they form an alliance*: The allies agreed to fight against God and his people. The nations named in this alliance lived around Israel and Judah.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:5-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Ishmaelites (ID: `group:Ishmael`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Amalek (ID: `place:Amalek`); Gebal (ID: `place:Gebal`); Hagri (ID: `person:Hagri`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`)

## Psalms 83:6–7 (id: 24979)

* The Edomites were descendants of Esau. They lived southeast of Judah (see [Genesis 25:30](https://ref.ly/Gen25:30); [32:3](https://ref.ly/Gen32:3); [36:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen36:1-Gen36:8)).
* The Ishmaelites were descendants of Hagar. They lived in the desert.
* The Moabites and Ammonites were descendants of Lot. They lived east of Israel and Judah.
* The Hagrites might have lived north of the Ammonites ([1 Chronicles 5:10](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Ishmaelites (ID: `group:Ishmael`); Ishmael (ID: `person:Ishmael`); Amalek (ID: `place:Amalek`); Gebal (ID: `place:Gebal`); Hagri (ID: `person:Hagri`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`)

## Psalms 83:7 (id: 24980)

* Gebal, also known as Byblos, was on the coast of Lebanon.
* The Amalekites lived south of Israel in the Negev desert.
* Philistia was southwest of Judah, near the Mediterranean Sea.
* Tyre was a port city on the Mediterranean Sea, northwest of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ammon (ID: `place:Ammon`); Amalek (ID: `place:Amalek`); Gebal (ID: `place:Gebal`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`)

## Psalms 83:8 (id: 24981)

* Assyria was located northeast of Israel and north of the Persian Gulf.
* Lot's descendants were the Moabites and the Ammonites ([Psalm 83:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps83:6-Ps83:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Lot (ID: `person:Lot`)

## Psalms 83:9–11 (id: 24982)

The Midianites, led by Oreb and Zeeb and ruled by Zebah and Zalmunna, were Israel's enemies during Gideon's time ([Judges 7:1–8:5](https://ref.ly/Judg7:1-Judg8:5)). Barak's alliance defeated Sisera and Jabin ([Judges 4](https://ref.ly/Judg4:1-Judg4:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zeeb (ID: `person:Zeeb`); Jabin (ID: `person:Jabin.2`); Sisera (ID: `person:Sisera`); Zebah (ID: `person:Zebah`); Zalmunna (ID: `person:Zalmunna`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Endor (ID: `place:En-dor`); Oreb (ID: `person:Oreb`); Kishon (ID: `place:Kishon`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 83:9-18

## Psalms 84:1–12 (id: 24984)

The psalmist deeply longs for God's presence. He feels weak with longing as he thinks about the temple and the pilgrims traveling to Jerusalem ([Psalm 84:1–7](https://ref.ly/Ps84:1-Ps84:7)). He prays for himself and the community ([Psalm 84:8–9](https://ref.ly/Ps84:8-Ps84:9)). 

The end of the psalm ([Psalm 84:10–12](https://ref.ly/Ps84:10-Ps84:12)) shows that the temple represents God. The psalmist truly desires God's presence. He understands that God's goodness is better than life and that only God can grant his people favor and honor ([Psalm 84:10–11](https://ref.ly/Ps84:10-Ps84:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 84:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Valley of Baca (ID: `place:BacaValley`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Swallow (ID: `fauna:Swallow`); Sparrow (ID: `fauna:Sparrow`)

Travelers needed strength to meet the challenges of pilgrimage by foot along unpaved paths, in all kinds of weather and with the possibility of assaults.

## Psalms 84:5–7 (id: 24990)

The pilgrimage to Jerusalem reminded people of the exodus, when God provided for his people in a wonderful way (see [Psalm 78:15–16](https://ref.ly/Ps78:15-Ps78:16); [105:41](https://ref.ly/Ps105:41)). The psalmist describes how the hardships of the journey turn into joy when the travelers arrive.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 84:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Valley of Baca (ID: `place:BacaValley`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 84:6 (id: 24992)

* The *Valley of Baca* is also called the Valley of Weeping or the Valley of Tears. It likely symbolizes the sadness that lonely pilgrims felt (see [Psalm 23:4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4)), rather than a real place.
* *spring … autumn rain*: Even when pilgrims feel completely worn out, the hope of getting closer to the Lord encourages them (compare [Isaiah 35:6–10](https://ref.ly/Isa35:6-Isa35:10); [41:18–20](https://ref.ly/Isa41:18-Isa41:20); [43:17–20](https://ref.ly/Isa43:17-Isa43:20); [49:10](https://ref.ly/Isa49:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 84:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Valley of Baca (ID: `place:BacaValley`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 85:1–5 (id: 24995)

Though the psalmist gratefully acknowledges God’s goodness, he also asks God whether his wrath will last forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 85:1–13 (id: 24994)

The psalmist leads the community that returned from exile in a lament and a prayer for complete restoration:

* He remembers how God rescued Israel from exile ([Psalm 85:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps85:1-Ps85:3)),
* asks for clearer signs of God’s goodness, and
* leads the people in prayer for full renewal ([Psalm 85:4–7](https://ref.ly/Ps85:4-Ps85:7)).

God answers with a message of peace ([Psalm 85:8–9](https://ref.ly/Ps85:8-Ps85:9)). The psalm ends with a poetic description of the greatness of God’s salvation ([85:10–13](https://ref.ly/Ps85:10-Ps85:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Psalms 85:2 (id: 24999)

When God forgave the guilt of their sins*,* he gave the people the possibility of fellowship with him (see [Psalm 32:5](https://ref.ly/Ps32:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Psalms 85:8–9 (id: 25001)

* When God speaks, his faithful people listen. He promises them peace, meaning wholeness and well\-being (see [Psalm 37:11](https://ref.ly/Ps37:11)). They respect the Lord and know that he and his salvation are near.
* The Lord does not tolerate foolish ways*,* such as those that resulted in the exile.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 85:10–13 (id: 24996)

In the renewed world of salvation, all the qualities of God’s care (love, truth, righteousness, and peace) come together. They transform creation and make it new (see [Isaiah 32:15–20](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15-Isa32:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 85:13 (id: 24997)

Righteousness is like a messenger preparing the world for God’s coming in response to the prayers of his people ([Psalms 85:4–7](https://ref.ly/Ps85:4-Ps85:7); [89:14](https://ref.ly/Ps89:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 85:13

## Psalms 86:1–17 (id: 25002)

The psalmist cries out to God for help and praises God’s character while facing evil ([Psalm 86:14](https://ref.ly/Ps86:14), [17](https://ref.ly/Ps86:17)). He says that all nations will one day worship the gracious and powerful God ([Psalm 86:8–10](https://ref.ly/Ps86:8-Ps86:10)). Because of this hope, the psalmist commits himself to live in a right and honest way ([Psalm 86:11](https://ref.ly/Ps86:11)) and to praise the Lord ([86:12–13](https://ref.ly/Ps86:12-Ps86:13)). 

The final section contrasts the behavior of evil people with the character of the Lord ([Psalm 86:14–17](https://ref.ly/Ps86:14-Ps86:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 86:1-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty.2`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); David (ID: `person:David`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`)

## Psalms 86:15 (id: 25007)

God shows mercy to his people ([Psalm 51:1](https://ref.ly/Ps51:1)). God is compassionate ([Psalm 25:6](https://ref.ly/Ps25:6)). God is slow to become angry ([Psalms 103:8](https://ref.ly/Ps103:8); [145:8](https://ref.ly/Ps145:8)). God is full of loving devotion and faithfulness (Hebrew *khesed* and *’emeth*; [Psalm 143:1](https://ref.ly/Ps143:1))

This verse repeats a theme first expressed in [Exodus 34:7](https://ref.ly/Exod34:7); [Numbers 14:18](https://ref.ly/Num14:18) (compare[Psalms 103:8](https://ref.ly/Ps103:8); [145:8](https://ref.ly/Ps145:8); [Nehemiah 9:17](https://ref.ly/Neh9:17); [Joel 2:13](https://ref.ly/Joel2:13); [Jonah 4:2](https://ref.ly/Jonah4:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 86:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 88:1–18 (id: 25016)

In this lament, the psalmist sees himself as cast out from God’s presence. At first, he calls on the Lord. When he finds no relief from his suffering, he presents three charges against God ([Psalm 88:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps88:6-Ps88:7), [8–12](https://ref.ly/Ps88:8-Ps88:12), [13–17](https://ref.ly/Ps88:13-Ps88:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 88:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abaddon (ID: `place:Abaddon`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Terror (ID: `keyterm:Terror`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Psalms 88:6–7 (id: 25021)

* In his first complaint, the psalmist blames the Lord for his suffering. God’s overwhelming anger does not make sense to him.
* The phrase *the darkest of the depths* might refer to:

    + the place of the dead ([Psalm 143:3](https://ref.ly/Ps143:3); see [Lamentations 3:6](https://ref.ly/Lam3:6)), or
        + the deep sea ([Psalms 68:22](https://ref.ly/Ps68:22); [69:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps69:1-Ps69:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 88:6-7


## Psalms 88:8–12 (id: 25022)

In his second charge, the psalmist claims that God has isolated him and caused his loneliness (compare [Psalms 31:11](https://ref.ly/Ps31:11); [38:11](https://ref.ly/Ps38:11)). In that culture, loneliness was a curse because relationships mattered more than personal success.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 88:8-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abaddon (ID: `place:Abaddon`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Psalms 88:13–17 (id: 25018)

In his third charge, the psalmist accuses the Lord of rejecting him. The force and apparent cruelty of the psalmist’s suffering overwhelm him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 88:13-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Terror (ID: `keyterm:Terror`)

## Psalms 88:18 (id: 25019)

The psalmist restates his second and first charges.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 88:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Psalms 89:1 (id: 25029)

God’s *loving devotion* gives reason to celebrate forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 89:1–52 (id: 25023)

Book Three ([Psalms 73–89](https://ref.ly/Ps73:1-Ps89:52)) starts and ends with serious questions. [Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52) begins with praise for how the Lord established David’s throne and kingdom ([89:1–37](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:37)). But later the psalmist protests that the Lord seems to have rejected his covenant with David ([Psalm 89:38–51](https://ref.ly/Ps89:38-Ps89:51); see [2 Samuel 7:8–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8-2Sam7:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:1-52

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

God is the absolute standard of faithfulness, commitment, and order. The angelic beings and the heavens testify to his constancy.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:5-8

## Psalms 89:9–10 (id: 25047)

* The Lord rules over all parts of the created order, including evil.
* *You scattered Your enemies*: The Lord will defeat ([Luke 1:51](https://ref.ly/Luke1:51)) evil forces that oppose him ([Psalm 2:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:3)).
* *with Your mighty arm*: This phrase represents God’s strength ([Psalm 89:13](https://ref.ly/Ps89:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:9-10


## Psalms 89:9–13 (id: 25048)

In this hymn, the psalmist celebrates God’s complete and powerful rule over nature.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mount Hermon (ID: `place:HermonMount`); Mount Tabor (ID: `place:TaborMount`)

Mount Tabor was in northern Israel. Mount Hermon formed the extreme northern border of the nation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mount Hermon (ID: `place:HermonMount`); Mount Tabor (ID: `place:TaborMount`)

## Psalms 89:14–18 (id: 25025)

God rules wisely over creation. The qualities of righteousness, justice, and faithfulness fit the description of a wise ruler. The happy people who live under his rule enjoy his protection.

## Psalms 89:20 (id: 25030)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:20

## Psalms 89:26–29 (id: 25031)

The Lord adopted David as his son, appointed him as his commander over the nations, and promised to support him forever. Verse 26 recalls a verse from David’s song of praise ([2 Samuel 22:47](https://ref.ly/2Sam22:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 89:27 (id: 25032)

David would become the greatest of earthly kings. The Hebrew word translated *highest* is the same word used for God as “Most High” (*‘elyon*).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:27


## Psalms 89:29 (id: 25033)

The Lord displays his faithfulness in the heavens, across his whole creation (see [Psalm 89:2](https://ref.ly/Ps89:2)). In the same way, he confirms his commitment to the kings that would come from David's family.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:29


## Psalms 89:30–37 (id: 25035)

The Lord disciplines his children when they go astray, but he does not reject them. He knows they will break their trust and will need correction, yet he remains committed to keep his covenant. His faithfulness to David is like his faithfulness to the created world.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:30-37

## Psalms 89:35 (id: 25036)

*holiness*: The Lord is completely different from the created world ([Psalm 22:3](https://ref.ly/Ps22:3)). His character is entirely pure. He acts according to his will ([Psalm 115:3](https://ref.ly/Ps115:3)) and chooses to be faithful ([89:1](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1); [115:1](https://ref.ly/Ps115:1)). Therefore, he cannot lie.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`)

## Psalms 89:38 (id: 25037)

*You are enraged*: The psalmist is confused by the severity of God's anger.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`)

## Psalms 89:40 (id: 25039)

*broken down all his walls*: The Lord’s people are completely vulnerable.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:40


## Psalms 89:42 (id: 25040)

Although the Lord promised to strengthen David, he has made David’s enemies strong

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:42


## Psalms 89:46–47 (id: 25041)

The psalmist wonders whether he will live long enough to see the Lord’s anger end. Human life is short and passes quickly before the Lord ([Psalm 90:4](https://ref.ly/Ps90:4), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps90:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:46-47

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 89:50 (id: 25043)

The phrase *Your servants* refers to the community loyal to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 89:52 (id: 25044)

This doxology (a short praise to God) ends Book Three of the Psalms ([Psalms 73–89](https://ref.ly/Ps73:1-Ps89:52)). It was not meant to be read as part of [Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52). Its words of praise stand in strong contrast to the pain and questions expressed in [Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52). Compare [Psalm 41:13](https://ref.ly/Ps41:13); [106:48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:48).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 89:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 90:1 (id: 25075)

* Moses led God's people from slavery in Egypt to Mount Sinai. There, he received the law and established God's covenant with Israel.
* *the man of God*: Moses had a special relationship with God ([Deuteronomy 33:1](https://ref.ly/Deut33:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 90:1–2 (id: 25068)

The Lord is the *dwelling place* for the godly. He has provided shelter for many generations. The word translated as *dwelling place* is rare in the Psalms. It describes a safe place where the Lord meets and cares for those in need ([Psalms 68:5](https://ref.ly/Ps68:5); [71:3](https://ref.ly/Ps71:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Psalms 90:1–17 (id: 25067)

Although the Lord’s people have lived in him for many generations, their sin creates distance between them and their eternal Lord. The Lord’s anger against human sin can seem to last a lifetime. Even then, it is only a small sign of his full displeasure with sin. This sad situation leads the community to seek the Lord’s light.

The first step is to ask the Lord to restore them because of his compassion and faithful love ([Psalm 90:13–14](https://ref.ly/Ps90:13-Ps90:14)). He alone can make his servants glad. He alone can bless the generations that come after them ([Psalm 90:16–17](https://ref.ly/Ps90:16-Ps90:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:1-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 90:2 (id: 25076)

* The Lord is the eternal God.
* The Lord created the earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 90:3–6 (id: 25077)

Unlike God, humans are mortal and temporary.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:3-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 90:7–10 (id: 25078)

Sinners cannot escape God’s anger against sin because the Lord sees their sins. Their lives pass quickly and are full of suffering.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Psalms 90:9–10 (id: 25079)

* A groan of sorrow ([Ezekiel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Ezek2:10)) represents a life facing the consequences of sin.
* Humans create trouble (see [Psalm 10:6](https://ref.ly/Ps10:6)) and receive pain as a result.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`)

## Psalms 90:11–12 (id: 25069)

In the search for wisdom, no one can fully understand the Lord (see [Isaiah 40:13](https://ref.ly/Isa40:13)). The right human response to the Lord is godly fear (see [Psalm 60:4](https://ref.ly/Ps60:4)). The Lord alone can teach humans to follow the path of wisdom ([Psalm 25:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps25:4-Ps25:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 90:13 (id: 25071)

The phrase *Your servants* refers to the community of those loyal to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 90:13–17 (id: 25070)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:13-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 90:15 (id: 25072)

The psalmist calls upon the Lord to give the people gladness to replace their mourning (see [Psalm 92:4–5](https://ref.ly/Ps92:4-Ps92:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:15


## Psalms 90:16 (id: 25073)

The *children* represent future generations in contrast to the generations past ([Psalm 90:1](https://ref.ly/Ps90:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:16


## Psalms 90:17 (id: 25074)

Humans waste their efforts unless the Lord makes them successful ([Psalm 44:3](https://ref.ly/Ps44:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 90:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

The *Most High* is an ancient title that expresses the Lord’s exalted status as the ruler and protector of the godly (see [Genesis 14:19](https://ref.ly/Gen14:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

The Lord is an ancient shelter (see [Psalm 90:1](https://ref.ly/Ps90:1)), open to those who seek refuge in him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:1-2

## Psalms 91:1–16 (id: 25080)

This wisdom psalm expresses confidence in the Almighty God, who provides a shelter for those who take refuge in him. They receive redemption, life, and glory from the Lord, who loves and cares for those who seek him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:1-16

## Psalms 91:4 (id: 25090)

The *shield* might refer to a large shield that covers the body. The *rampart* likely refers to a smaller shield, though the exact meaning of the Hebrew word is uncertain.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Large Shield (ID: `realia:LargeShield`)

## Psalms 91:5–8 (id: 25091)

The godly should not be afraid*,* because the Lord watches over them. These promises do not guarantee an escape from trouble, but they create an expectation of the Lord’s goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:5-8

The word *pestilence* here means any kind of suffering or trouble.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:9-13

## Psalms 91:11 (id: 25082)

*He will command His angels*: Satan quoted these words when he tempted Jesus ([Matthew 4:6](https://ref.ly/Matt4:6); [Luke 4:10–11](https://ref.ly/Luke4:10-Luke4:11)). However, God makes this promise for those who obey him ([Psalm 91:14](https://ref.ly/Ps91:14)), not for those who arrogantly test him ([Matthew 4:4](https://ref.ly/Matt4:4), [7](https://ref.ly/Matt4:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 91:11

## Psalms 92:1 (id: 25101)

In Jewish tradition, each day of the week has an assigned psalm: 

* Sunday ([Psalm 24](https://ref.ly/Ps24:1-Ps24:10))
* Monday ([Psalm 48](https://ref.ly/Ps48:1-Ps48:14))
* Tuesday ([Psalm 82](https://ref.ly/Ps82:1-Ps82:8))
* Wednesday ([Psalm 94](https://ref.ly/Ps94:1-Ps94:23))
* Thursday ([Psalm 81](https://ref.ly/Ps81:1-Ps81:16))
* Friday ([Psalm 93](https://ref.ly/Ps93:1-Ps93:5))
* Saturday, which is the Sabbath ([Psalm 92](https://ref.ly/Ps92:1-Ps92:15))

This is the only psalm that indicates this use in its title.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 92:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 92:1–3 (id: 25095)

The psalmist offers praise and thanksgiving for God’s loving devotion and faithfulness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 92:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`)

## Psalms 92:1–15 (id: 25094)

This psalm begins with thanksgiving and praise. The Lord, the Most High, is fair and just in judging the godly and the wicked. The godly will have a bright and strong future, while the wicked will die. 

Wise people see and understand the Lord's actions, which show his justice ([Psalm 92:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps92:6-Ps92:7), [11](https://ref.ly/Ps92:11), [15](https://ref.ly/Ps92:15)). The wicked, like grass, are quickly destroyed ([Psalm 92:7–10](https://ref.ly/Ps92:7-Ps92:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 92:1-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Stone Pine (ID: `flora:Pine`); Horn (ID: `keyterm:Horn`); Stupid (ID: `keyterm:Stupid`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Wild Ox (ID: `fauna:WildOx`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 92:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 92:8 (id: 25107)

The Lord is above the world of human experience. From this exalted place, he rules and judges ([Psalm 7:7](https://ref.ly/Ps7:7)). From there, he:

* celebrates his victories ([Psalm 68:18](https://ref.ly/Ps68:18); see [47](https://ref.ly/Ps47:1-Ps47:9)),
* comes to rescue the needy ([Psalm 102:19](https://ref.ly/Ps102:19)),

* receives praise ([Psalm 148:1](https://ref.ly/Ps148:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 92:8

Carvings of palm trees ([1 Kings 6:29](https://ref.ly/1Kgs6:29), [32](https://ref.ly/1Kgs6:32), [35](https://ref.ly/1Kgs6:35); [7:36](https://ref.ly/1Kgs7:36)) decorated the temple. ## Psalms 93:1–100.5 (id: 25108)

These psalms challenge the doubts created by the exile (see [Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52)) and answer the questions asked in [Psalm 90:11](https://ref.ly/Ps90:11), [13](https://ref.ly/Ps90:13). The Lord has established a kingdom ([Psalm 93](https://ref.ly/Ps93:1-Ps93:5)) that is characterized by: 

* punishment of the wicked ([Psalm 94](https://ref.ly/Ps94:1-Ps94:23)),
* respectful obedience among his people ([Psalm 95](https://ref.ly/Ps95:1-Ps95:11)),
* justice for the poor ([Psalm 96](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:13)),
* celebration in Zion ([Psalm 97](https://ref.ly/Ps97:1-Ps97:12)),
* salvation for Israel ([Psalm 98](https://ref.ly/Ps98:1-Ps98:9)),
* holiness ([Psalm 99](https://ref.ly/Ps99:1-Ps99:9)), and
* praise ([Psalm 100](https://ref.ly/Ps100:1-Ps100:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 93:1-100:5

## Psalms 94:1–23 (id: 25113)

This plea for the Lord’s justice includes both communal and individual expressions of sorrow ([Psalm 94:1–7](https://ref.ly/Ps94:1-Ps94:7), [16–23](https://ref.ly/Ps94:16-Ps94:23)). The psalmist speaks about the pain and suffering caused by the wicked. He also describes the comfort and stability of the Lord’s protection. He calls on the Lord to avenge the needy against the arrogant and foolish, he rebukes such people for their foolishness ([Psalm 94:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps94:8-Ps94:11)), and speaks a blessing on the wise ([94:12–15](https://ref.ly/Ps94:12-Ps94:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 94:1-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Stupid (ID: `keyterm:Stupid`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Declare Guilty (ID: `keyterm:DeclareGuilty`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 94:10 (id: 25115)

*discipline*: This word can also mean "admonished" ([Psalm 2:10](https://ref.ly/Ps2:10)), "discipline" ([6:1](https://ref.ly/Ps6:1); [94:12](https://ref.ly/Ps94:12)), or "instructs" ([16:7](https://ref.ly/Ps16:7)). The Lord disciplines his people to improve their character ([Proverbs 3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Prov3:11-Prov3:12); [Hebrews 12:6–11](https://ref.ly/Heb12:6-Heb12:11)). He punishes his enemies by giving them what they deserve ([Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6); [Romans 6:23](https://ref.ly/Rom6:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 95:9


## Psalms 95:11 (id: 25129)

* The generation of Israel that wandered in the wilderness for 40 years did not enter the place of rest that Israel enjoyed after conquering the land ([1 Kings 8:56](https://ref.ly/1Kgs8:56); see [Deuteronomy 12:9](https://ref.ly/Deut12:9)). The Lord promised them the land and gave it to them ([Isaiah 28:12](https://ref.ly/Isa28:12)), but they did not obey him ([Isaiah 28:1](https://ref.ly/Isa28:1)). This warning reminds every generation of the need to obey with faith.
* The rest that God provides includes peace, provision, and protection ([Psalm 132:14–18](https://ref.ly/Ps132:14-Ps132:18)). The author of Hebrews challenged a new generation to enter the rest provided by Jesus Christ ([Hebrews 3:7–4:13](https://ref.ly/Heb3:7-Heb4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 95:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`)

## Psalms 96:1 (id: 25141)

Three psalms begin by urging people to sing a new song ([Psalms 96](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:13), [98](https://ref.ly/Ps98:1-Ps98:9), [149](https://ref.ly/Ps149:1-Ps149:9)). The song celebrates the Lord's universal kingship (see [Psalm 33:3](https://ref.ly/Ps33:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:1–13 (id: 25137)

* This psalm establishes the Lord’s universal kingship (see [Psalm 93](https://ref.ly/Ps93:1-Ps93:5)) and prepares for the coming of the Lord to bring justice and righteousness to the world. The psalmist invites everyone to worship the Lord ([Psalm 96:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:3)), the glorious Creator of the earth ([96:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps96:4-Ps96:6)), because only he deserves worship ([96:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps96:7-Ps96:9)). The universe joins in praise as the righteous Judge establishes a new order ([Psalm 96:10–13](https://ref.ly/Ps96:10-Ps96:13)).
* This psalm (along with [Psalm 105:1–15](https://ref.ly/Ps105:1-Ps105:15); [106:1](https://ref.ly/Ps106:1), [47–48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:47-Ps106:48)) is included in David’s song of praise recorded in [1 Chronicles 16:1–43](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:1-1Chr16:43).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:1-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 96:4 (id: 25143)

*great is the LORD … greatly to be praised*: The poet praises the Lord above all gods ([Psalm 95:3](https://ref.ly/Ps95:3)) and nations ([99:2](https://ref.ly/Ps99:2)). The Lord inspires more awe than anyone or anything else ([Psalm 76:7](https://ref.ly/Ps76:7), [12](https://ref.ly/Ps76:12); [89:7](https://ref.ly/Ps89:7); [99:3](https://ref.ly/Ps99:3); [111:9](https://ref.ly/Ps111:9)). He is beyond human possession or comprehension. The story of what he does to redeem his people causes them to praise him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:4–6 (id: 25142)

The gods of the nations are as nothing before the glorious and awesome Creator of the earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Psalms 96:5 (id: 25144)

The Lord made the heavens*,* an even greater task than creating the earth. The idols*,* made by human hands ([Psalm 115:4](https://ref.ly/Ps115:4)), created nothing.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Psalms 96:6 (id: 25145)

*Splendor and majesty*: The Lord is glorious ([Psalm 96:6](https://ref.ly/Ps96:6); [104:1](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1); [148:13](https://ref.ly/Ps148:13)), and so are his works in nature ([8:1](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1)) and in redemption ([111:3](https://ref.ly/Ps111:3); [145:5](https://ref.ly/Ps145:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`)

## Psalms 96:7 (id: 25146)

* *families of the nations*: Families and clans represent subdivisions of the nations (see [Genesis 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).
* The nations must respond to the Lord’s glory ([Psalms 8:1](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1); [19:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:4); [24:7](https://ref.ly/Ps24:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:8 (id: 25147)

The courts of God’s sanctuary were usually not open to non\-Jewish people. In this psalm, the nations of the world (people who are not Jews) are invited to come in ([Psalm 65:4](https://ref.ly/Ps65:4); [84:2](https://ref.ly/Ps84:2), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps84:10); [100:4](https://ref.ly/Ps100:4); [116:19](https://ref.ly/Ps116:19); [135:2](https://ref.ly/Ps135:2); [Isaiah 1:12](https://ref.ly/Isa1:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:9 (id: 25148)

Worshipers should tremble in awe ([Psalm 96:4](https://ref.ly/Ps96:4); see [18:7–15](https://ref.ly/Ps18:7-Ps18:15); [97:4](https://ref.ly/Ps97:4)) at the splendor of the Lord’s great power and holiness ([Hebrews 12:25](https://ref.ly/Heb12:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:10 (id: 25138)

* The Lord’s coming means the end of injustice. He rules fairly ([Psalm 9:8](https://ref.ly/Ps9:8); [96:13](https://ref.ly/Ps96:13); [98:9](https://ref.ly/Ps98:9)). He brings a time of peace and order like the world has not known since Adam and Eve lived in the garden of Eden.
* The Lord’s rule over the world stands on the strong pillars of “righteousness and justice” ([Psalm 89:14](https://ref.ly/Ps89:14)), so it cannot be shaken (compare [11:3](https://ref.ly/Ps11:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 96:11–13 (id: 25139)

The psalmist urges all of creation to respond with joy to the news of the Lord’s coming. Compare a similar theme in [Psalm 98:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps98:7-Ps98:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 96:12 (id: 25140)

All of nature will experience renewal in God’s presence ([Psalms 65:13](https://ref.ly/Ps65:13); [72:6](https://ref.ly/Ps72:6),[16](https://ref.ly/Ps72:16); [Isaiah 44:23](https://ref.ly/Isa44:23); [55:12](https://ref.ly/Isa55:12); [Romans 8:21–22](https://ref.ly/Rom8:21-Rom8:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 96:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 97:1 (id: 25151)

The *distant shores* represent the most remote parts of the world.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 97:1–12 (id: 25149)

God is greatly exalted over the gods and over all the earth. Awe\-inspiring phenomena accompany his coming. Even the created order is visibly affected and the wicked perish, but the people of Zion love his coming and rejoice.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Psalms 97:2–4 (id: 25152)

*Clouds … Fire … lightning*: This language shows that the Lord is coming to judge.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:2-4


## Psalms 97:5–7 (id: 25153)

All creation responds to the coming of its King. He alone deserves worship.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Psalms 97:6 (id: 25154)

* When the Lord comes into the world, the heavens announce his righteousness ([Psalm 50:6](https://ref.ly/Ps50:6)).
* Every nation will see the clear display of the Lord’s glory (see [Psalm 8:1](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1); [19:1–4a](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:4); [24:7](https://ref.ly/Ps24:7); [Isaiah 40:5](https://ref.ly/Isa40:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Psalms 97:7 (id: 25155)

At the Lord’s coming, when every god must bow to him, those who worship other gods will face their own foolishness (see [Psalm 95:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps95:3-Ps95:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Psalms 97:8–12 (id: 25156)

Jerusalem rejoices at the news of the Lord’s coming, for he brings justice and rescues the *upright of heart*.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:8-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Psalms 97:11 (id: 25150)

Godly people who live with integrity (see [Proverbs 2:7](https://ref.ly/Prov2:7), [21](https://ref.ly/Prov2:21); [3:32](https://ref.ly/Prov3:32); [11:6](https://ref.ly/Prov11:6), [11](https://ref.ly/Prov11:11); [14:2](https://ref.ly/Prov14:2)) have hearts that are right before the Lord. They maintain a relationship with the Lord and live rightly with others ([Psalms 94:15](https://ref.ly/Ps94:15); [125:4](https://ref.ly/Ps125:4)). 

The wicked plan to destroy the godly ([Psalm 11:2](https://ref.ly/Ps11:2)), but the Lord rescues them ([11:1](https://ref.ly/Ps11:1); [36:10](https://ref.ly/Ps36:10)). Then the sorrows of the godly turn into joy and praise ([Psalms 32:11](https://ref.ly/Ps32:11); [64:10](https://ref.ly/Ps64:10); [111:1](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1); [119:7](https://ref.ly/Ps119:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 97:11


## Psalms 98:1–9 (id: 25157)

This psalm celebrates the Lord as King. The Lord is the true King of Israel, its champion and savior. His people sing a new song to celebrate his victory and his faithfulness to them.

The Lord shows his righteous rule to all the nations of the earth. One day, this will lead to a worldwide kingdom of justice ([Psalm 98:2](https://ref.ly/Ps98:2), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps98:9)). Because of this, all the earth should worship and rejoice together, for the Lord is coming ([Psalm 98:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps98:4-Ps98:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 98:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Trumpet (ID: `realia:Trumpet`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`)

## Psalms 98:2–3 (id: 25158)

The Lord makes known his righteousness and faithfulness (see also [Isaiah 52:10](https://ref.ly/Isa52:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 98:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Psalms 98:4–6 (id: 25159)

The psalmist invites the whole earth to worship the King (see [Psalm 96:1](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 98:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`); Trumpet (ID: `realia:Trumpet`)

The temple musicians played trumpets to gather people for meetings (compare [Psalm 81:3](https://ref.ly/Ps81:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 98:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Trumpet (ID: `realia:Trumpet`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`)

## Psalms 98:7–9 (id: 25161)

The psalm speaks as if all creation rejoices when the righteous Judge comes (see [Psalms 83:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps93:3-Ps93:4); [96:11–13](https://ref.ly/Ps96:11-Ps96:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 98:7-9

## Psalms 99:1 (id: 25164)

* It is appropriate to tremble and quake in response to the Lord’s holiness (see [Psalm 96:9](https://ref.ly/Ps96:9)).
* The throne between the cherubim refers to the cover of the ark of the Testimony ([Exodus 25:17–22](https://ref.ly/Exod25:17-Exod25:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`)

## Psalms 99:1–5 (id: 25162)

The Lord is the holy and righteous King ([Isaiah 6:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1-Isa6:5)). Mount Zion, where the temple of the Lord is located, metaphorically stands as the highest point on earth ([Psalm 113:4](https://ref.ly/Ps113:4); [Isaiah 2:2](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2); [6:1](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1)). From this high place, the "great King" ([Psalm 47:2](https://ref.ly/Ps47:2)) can see, rule over, and receive praise from all nations and peoples.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`)

## Psalms 99:1–9 (id: 25163)

The psalmist calls the nations to worship the Lord, the king of the earth who rules from Zion.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Samuel (ID: `person:Samuel`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`)

## Psalms 99:4 (id: 25165)

The qualities of the Lord’s rule include: 

* strength,
* justice,
* love,
* fairness, and
* righteousness (see [Psalms 93:4](https://ref.ly/Ps93:4); [97:2](https://ref.ly/Ps97:2), [6–7](https://ref.ly/Ps97:6-Ps97:7), [12](https://ref.ly/Ps97:12); [98:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps98:2-Ps98:3), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps98:9); [138:6](https://ref.ly/Ps138:6)).

## Psalms 99:6 (id: 25167)

Moses and Samuel were great models and leaders who had prayed on behalf of the Lord’s people ([Exodus 32:31–32](https://ref.ly/Exod32:31-Exod32:32); [1 Samuel 12:23](https://ref.ly/1Sam12:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Samuel (ID: `person:Samuel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 99:6–8 (id: 25166)

God showed favor by responding to his people in the past, while holding sinners accountable.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:6-8

## Psalms 99:7 (id: 25168)

The Lord spoke to Moses and Aaron from *the pillar of cloud* ([Exodus 33:9](https://ref.ly/Exod33:9); [Numbers 12:5–6](https://ref.ly/Num12:5-Num12:6)). For a while, the people responded to the revelation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 99:7

## Psalms 100:1–5 (id: 23673)

This psalm calls all nations to come to Jerusalem to worship the Lord and acknowledge his goodness to Israel.
