## Psalms 100:3 (id: 23674)

* The Lord is the one who determines who his people are.
* *the sheep of His pasture*: God will guide and protect his people ([Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1), [4](https://ref.ly/Ps23:4)).

Gates and courts were part of the temple in Jerusalem.

## Psalms 101:1–8 (id: 23676)

This song of praise expresses a commitment to live with integrity and to avoid evil. Jesus meets this ideal.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 101:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Wickedness (ID: `keyterm:Wickedness`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); David (ID: `person:David`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 101:2 (id: 23677)

* *in my house*: This commitment to integrity might refer to private life at home. Some interpreters see *my house* as the king’s palace. The commitment would then refer to the policies of his administration.
* Living with integrity means choosing a wise lifestyle.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 101:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Psalms 101:4–5 (id: 23678)

* The psalmist hates what the Lord hates ([Proverbs 6:16–19](https://ref.ly/Prov6:16-Prov6:19); see [Psalms 5:5](https://ref.ly/Ps5:5); [11:4–5](https://ref.ly/Ps11:4-Ps11:5)).
* Perverse ideas come from corrupt human hearts (compare [Psalm 18:26](https://ref.ly/Ps18:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 101:4-5


## Psalms 101:6 (id: 23679)

The psalmist surrounds himself with people who are also committed to integrity ([Psalm 101:2](https://ref.ly/Ps101:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 101:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Psalms 101:7–8 (id: 23680)

The psalmist promises to remove all evil from his house and the city of the Lord, which represents the entire kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 101:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 102:1–2 (id: 23682)

The psalmist asks the Lord not to turn away (or *hide your face*). Israel experienced God turning his face away when they were exiled.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:1–28 (id: 23681)

A weak and suffering person prays for the Lord's mercy on Jerusalem and for his presence with its people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:1-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl.2`); Jackdaw (ID: `fauna:Jackdaw`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Psalms 102:3 (id: 23698)

* *Smoke* is an image of the temporary nature of life ([Psalms 37:20](https://ref.ly/Ps37:20); [68:2](https://ref.ly/Ps68:2)).
* The psalmist’s *bones* represent his whole being (see [Psalms 6:2](https://ref.ly/Ps6:2); [34:19–22](https://ref.ly/Ps34:19-Ps34:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:3


## Psalms 102:4 (id: 23699)

Like *withered grass,* the psalmist senses his life fading away.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 102:5 (id: 23700)

*skin hangs on my bones*: The psalmist has become very thin due to his deep sorrow (see [Psalm 102:9](https://ref.ly/Ps102:9); [109:24](https://ref.ly/Ps109:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:5


## Psalms 102:6 (id: 23701)

Owls often live in deserted places, such as ruined cities. Because of this, the Bible sometimes uses the owl as a sign of ruin and desolation ([Isaiah 34:11](https://ref.ly/Isa34:11), [15](https://ref.ly/Isa34:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl.2`); Jackdaw (ID: `fauna:Jackdaw`)

## Psalms 102:7–8 (id: 23702)

The psalmist cannot sleep (see [Psalm 63:6–8](https://ref.ly/Ps63:6-Ps63:8)). Like *a lone bird on a housetop,* he feels alone and separated from both friends (see [Psalm 88:8](https://ref.ly/Ps88:8)) and enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Psalms 102:10 (id: 23683)

The psalmist blames the Lord for his troubles.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:10


## Psalms 102:11 (id: 23684)

The psalmist feels that life is empty and short ([Psalms 102:4](https://ref.ly/Ps102:4); [109:23](https://ref.ly/Ps109:23); [144:4](https://ref.ly/Ps144:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 102:12 (id: 23686)

*forever … to all generations*: Human life is short and fragile ([Psalm 102:3–11](https://ref.ly/Ps102:3-Ps102:11)). In contrast, the Lord remains forever and rules through every generation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:12–17 (id: 23685)

The topic changes from the psalmist’s troubles to the Lord’s glory. A vision of the Lord’s compassionate kingship gives him confidence in Zion’s future.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:12-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 102:13 (id: 23687)

Now is the time for the Lord to restore Jerusalem from exile (see [Psalm 75:2](https://ref.ly/Ps75:2); [Jeremiah 29:10](https://ref.ly/Jer29:10); [Daniel 9:1–19](https://ref.ly/Dan9:1-Dan9:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`)

## Psalms 102:15–16 (id: 23688)

When the Lord arises ([Psalm 102:13](https://ref.ly/Ps102:13)), the nations will tremble with fear because they will see his glory (see [Psalm 8:1](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1); [19:1–4a](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:4); [24:7](https://ref.ly/Ps24:7)). Rebuilding Zion and the temple shows God's glory and fulfills this promise. An even greater fulfillment will come with the New Jerusalem ([Revelation 21](https://ref.ly/Rev21:1-Rev21:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 102:17 (id: 23689)

The people of Israel were very poor and vulnerable (*destitute*) during the exile.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:17


## Psalms 102:18 (id: 23691)

*Let this be written*: The written record serves as a reminder of the Lord's goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:18–22 (id: 23690)

The Lord will show mercy. Both Jews and Gentiles will worship him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:18-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`)

## Psalms 102:19 (id: 23692)

*His sanctuary* refers to God's holy place in heaven (see [Psalm 92:8–10](https://ref.ly/Ps92:8-Ps92:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:22 (id: 23693)

Many people (both Jews and non\-Jews) will worship the Lord together (see [Psalm 96:7](https://ref.ly/Ps96:7), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps96:9); [106:48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:48); [Isaiah 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2-Isa2:4); [43:10–13](https://ref.ly/Isa43:10-Isa43:13); [57:15](https://ref.ly/Isa57:15); [60:3–7](https://ref.ly/Isa60:3-Isa60:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:23–28 (id: 23694)

Although the psalmist suffers in the present, he bases his hope for the future on the Lord’s past faithfulness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:23-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:24 (id: 23695)

The psalmist compares his short life ([Psalm 102:23](https://ref.ly/Ps102:23)) to God's eternal existence (compare [90:4](https://ref.ly/Ps90:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 102:25–27 (id: 23696)

* *wear out like a garment*: Unlike the eternal Creator, the created world will wear out like a piece of clothing (see [Isaiah 40:28](https://ref.ly/Isa40:28)).
* The writer to the Hebrews describes this passage as having been addressed to Jesus, the Son ([Hebrews 1:10–12](https://ref.ly/Heb1:10-Heb1:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:25-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Psalms 102:28 (id: 23697)

The Lord’s presence provides the basis for the security and happiness of his people ([Psalm 23:6](https://ref.ly/Ps23:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 102:28


## Psalms 103:1–2 (id: 23704)

*all that is within me, bless His holy name*: [Psalms 103](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:22) and [104](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1-Ps104:35) both start and end with this promise ([103:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:2), [22](https://ref.ly/Ps103:22); [104:1](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1), [35](https://ref.ly/Ps104:35); see also [146:1](https://ref.ly/Ps146:1)).

## Psalms 103:11–12 (id: 23706)

God’s love and forgiveness have no limits and are complete.

## Psalms 103:13 (id: 23707)

The Lord is like a father to his children. This idea is central to the teachings of Jesus about God's fatherhood (see [Matthew 5:43–48](https://ref.ly/Matt5:43-Matt5:48); [6:1](https://ref.ly/Matt6:1); [10:19–20](https://ref.ly/Matt10:19-Matt10:20); [12:50](https://ref.ly/Matt12:50); [Luke 6:36](https://ref.ly/Luke6:36); [12:29–32](https://ref.ly/Luke12:29-Luke12:32); [John 8:31–59](https://ref.ly/John8:31-John8:59); [15:1–8](https://ref.ly/John15:1-John15:8); see also [2 Samuel 7:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:14); [Jeremiah 3:19](https://ref.ly/Jer3:19); [31:9](https://ref.ly/Jer31:9); [Malachi 1:6](https://ref.ly/Mal1:6); [2 Corinthians 6:16–18](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:16-2Cor6:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 103:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 103:14–16 (id: 23708)

The Lord compassionately cares for weak humans (see [Psalm 37:2](https://ref.ly/Ps37:2); [90:5](https://ref.ly/Ps90:5); [92:7](https://ref.ly/Ps92:7); [102:4](https://ref.ly/Ps102:4); [Isaiah 40:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa40:6-Isa40:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 103:14-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 103:18 (id: 23710)

To obey God means to be faithful and loyal to him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 103:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Psalms 103:20–21 (id: 23711)

The psalmist urges the Lord's angels, servants, and messengers to praise the Lord.

## Psalms 104:1 (id: 23722)

* *Bless the LORD, O my soul!*: [Psalms 103](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:22) and [104](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1-Ps104:35) both begin and end with this commitment to praise ([103:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:2), [22](https://ref.ly/Ps103:22); [104:1](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1), [35](https://ref.ly/Ps104:35)).
* *You are very great*: These words praise God for rescuing his people (see also [Psalms 35:27](https://ref.ly/Ps35:27); [40:16](https://ref.ly/Ps40:16); [70:4](https://ref.ly/Ps70:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 104:1–35 (id: 23715)

This creation hymn (see also [Psalms 8](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1-Ps8:9), [33](https://ref.ly/Ps33:1-Ps33:22), [145](https://ref.ly/Ps145:1-Ps145:21)) praises God's goodness and greatness. The psalmist thinks about the current world, the original creation, and a future new creation. He views both creations as wonderfully and wisely made (compare [Psalm 139:14](https://ref.ly/Ps139:14)), as the work of the Lord's Spirit ([104:30](https://ref.ly/Ps104:30); [Genesis 1:2](https://ref.ly/Gen1:2); [2 Corinthians 3:6](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:1-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Hyrax (ID: `fauna:Hyrax`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Phoenician Juniper (ID: `flora:JuniperTree`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Grecian Juniper (ID: `flora:Juniper`); Ibex (ID: `fauna:Ibex`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Leviathan (ID: `fauna:GiantCroccodile`); Cypress (ID: `flora:Cypress`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Stork (ID: `fauna:Stork`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Fir (ID: `flora:Fir`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Linen Strips (ID: `realia:LinenStrips`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`); Goat Hair Strips (ID: `realia:GoatHairStrips`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 104:2–4 (id: 23723)

The psalmist refers to two days of creation (see [Genesis 1:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:8)), when God created the heavens and the light.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:2-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Linen Strips (ID: `realia:LinenStrips`); Goat Hair Strips (ID: `realia:GoatHairStrips`)

## Psalms 104:3 (id: 23734)

This poetic description pictures the clouds as *beams* that support the heavens.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Psalms 104:4 (id: 23735)

* Wind and lightning (*flames of fire*) are described as God’s messengers. The Lord controls the wind, rain, and storms and uses them as he desires ([Psalm 77:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps77:17-Ps77:18); [78:26](https://ref.ly/Ps78:26); [135:7](https://ref.ly/Ps135:7); [148:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps148:5-Ps148:6)).
* The psalmist refers to the third day of creation ([Genesis 1:9–13](https://ref.ly/Gen1:9-Gen1:13)). The Lord is master of water and earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Psalms 104:6 (id: 23736)

At first, water covered the earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:6


## Psalms 104:7 (id: 23737)

The Lord created dry ground on day three of creation ([Genesis 1:9–13](https://ref.ly/Gen1:9-Gen1:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:7


## Psalms 104:8 (id: 23738)

The earth was made because God willed it.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:8


## Psalms 104:9 (id: 23740)

The Lord set a firm boundary for the seas to protect the land. This shows that the Lord rules both the sea and the land. This idea also challenged the beliefs of the Canaanites, who celebrated Baal as the god who defeated the sea (see [Psalm 29](https://ref.ly/Ps29:1-Ps29:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:9


## Psalms 104:9–10 (id: 23739)

The Lord rules over all waters, including seas, rivers, and springs on the land. He brought order to the waters that were once chaotic. As a result, the earth became a rich place for animals and people. Water refreshes the ground and provides life for birds and other animals.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:9-10


## Psalms 104:11–18 (id: 23716)

The psalmist celebrates animal, plant, and human life, as well as the Lord’s abundant provision for all of it ([Genesis 1:9–30](https://ref.ly/Gen1:9-Gen1:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:11-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Hyrax (ID: `fauna:Hyrax`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); Phoenician Juniper (ID: `flora:JuniperTree`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Grecian Juniper (ID: `flora:Juniper`); Ibex (ID: `fauna:Ibex`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Cypress (ID: `flora:Cypress`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Stork (ID: `fauna:Stork`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Fir (ID: `flora:Fir`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`)

## Psalms 104:14 (id: 23717)

*for man to cultivate*: People are the managers of the created order, and they receive nourishment from the work of their hands.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 104:15 (id: 23718)

Both wine and olive oil can be misused (compare [Proverbs 21:17](https://ref.ly/Prov21:17)), but the Lord gives them as gifts to improve life. Life is sustained by basic food, represented by bread.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Psalms 104:17–18 (id: 23719)

* The storks migrated from northern Europe and stayed in Palestine during the winter.
* *rock badgers*: These animals are about the size of a rabbit and live in rocky areas.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:17-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hyrax (ID: `fauna:Hyrax`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Grecian Juniper (ID: `flora:Juniper`); Ibex (ID: `fauna:Ibex`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Cypress (ID: `flora:Cypress`); Stork (ID: `fauna:Stork`); Phoenician Juniper (ID: `flora:JuniperTree`); Fir (ID: `flora:Fir`)

## Psalms 104:19 (id: 23721)

* *the moon to mark the seasons*: Ancient Israel based its calendar on the cycles of the moon.
* *the sun knows when to set*: This poetic description reflects what people normally see. The sun goes down at a regular time each evening.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:19


## Psalms 104:19–23 (id: 23720)

The psalmist rejoices in the order of night and day, marking day four of creation ([Genesis 1:14–19](https://ref.ly/Gen1:14-Gen1:19)). This cycle gives all creatures an opportunity to live.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:19-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 104:20–23 (id: 23724)

Cycles of darkness and dawn mark the days on earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:20-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Psalms 104:24–26 (id: 23725)

The Lord’s works testify to his wisdom. All parts fit together (land and sea, night and day, land creatures and sea creatures). The Lord’s wisdom makes life orderly and wonderful.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Leviathan (ID: `fauna:GiantCroccodile`)

## Psalms 104:25 (id: 23726)

*teeming with creatures beyond number*: The ocean is full of all kinds of life (see [Genesis 1:20–21](https://ref.ly/Gen1:20-Gen1:21)). This shows the Lord's creative work.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:25


## Psalms 104:26 (id: 23727)

* *ships pass*: Commercial ships used for sea trade were one of the wonders of the ancient Near East (see [Isaiah 2:16](https://ref.ly/Isa2:16); [23:1](https://ref.ly/Isa23:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Isa23:3)).
* *Leviathan*: The psalmist describes this feared sea creature as something the Lord created and controls.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Leviathan (ID: `fauna:GiantCroccodile`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`)

## Psalms 104:27–30 (id: 23728)

All living things depend on the Lord, who provides what they need and who has power over life and death (compare [Matthew 6:11](https://ref.ly/Matt6:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:27-30


## Psalms 104:29–30 (id: 23729)

*When You send Your Spirit*: Life on earth is only possible as the Lord’s gift, symbolized by his breath (Hebrew *ruakh*, which means "breath, wind, or spirit"). The Lord’s Spirit is the agent of renewal ([Isaiah 32:15–20](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15-Isa32:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:29-30


## Psalms 104:31 (id: 23731)

The Lord reveals his presence in the created order (see [Psalms 8:1](https://ref.ly/Ps8:1); [19:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:4); [24:7](https://ref.ly/Ps24:7)) and delights in his new creation (see [Isaiah 65:19](https://ref.ly/Isa65:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 104:31–35 (id: 23730)

The psalmist moves from describing the known creation to anticipating the new creation, when the Lord will renew the created order into a world without sinners.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:31-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 104:32 (id: 23732)

The trembling earth and the smoking mountains represent the Lord’s coming in judgment against sinners and salvation for his faithful people (see[Psalm 18:7–15](https://ref.ly/Ps18:7-Ps18:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:32


## Psalms 104:35 (id: 23733)

* The Lord will remove all forms of evil that defile his creation.
* The final phrase returns to the opening words of [Psalm 104:1](https://ref.ly/Ps104:1) (see also [103:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps103:1-Ps103:2), [22](https://ref.ly/Ps103:22); [146:1](https://ref.ly/Ps146:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 104:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 105:1–5 (id: 23742)

The poet begins with a call to give thanks (see also [Psalms 106:1](https://ref.ly/Ps106:1); [107:1](https://ref.ly/Ps107:1); [118:1](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1); [136:1](https://ref.ly/Ps136:1)). The other psalms continue with the refrain "for He is good!" The rest of [Psalm 105](https://ref.ly/Ps105:1-Ps105:45) tells of God's goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 105:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`)

## Psalms 105:1–45 (id: 23741)

* This hymn praises the Lord’s faithfulness to Abraham and his descendants in all their journeys:

    + from Ur to Canaan ([Psalm 105:12](https://ref.ly/Ps105:12)),
        + to Egypt ([Psalm 105:17](https://ref.ly/Ps105:17), [23](https://ref.ly/Ps105:23)),
        + through the wilderness ([Psalm 105:37](https://ref.ly/Ps105:37), [41](https://ref.ly/Ps105:41)), and
        + back to Canaan ([Psalm 105:44](https://ref.ly/Ps105:44)).
        These events fulfilled the Lord's promise to Abraham ([Psalm 105:9](https://ref.ly/Ps105:9), [42](https://ref.ly/Ps105:42)), which was the basis for Israel’s very existence. Joseph, whose suffering turned into glory, serves as a model of encouragement for Israel ([Psalm 105:16–25](https://ref.ly/Ps105:16-Ps105:25)).

* [Psalm 105:1–15](https://ref.ly/Ps105:1-Ps105:15) (along with [96](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:13); [106:1](https://ref.ly/Ps106:1), [47–48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:47-Ps106:48)) is included in David’s song of praise recorded in [1 Chronicles 16](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:1-1Chr16:43).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 105:1-45; Psalms 96:1-13

## Psalms 105:6–11 (id: 23757)

The story of Israel is based on the Lord’s commitment to Abraham. Even when a question arises about the future of God’s covenant with David ([Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52)), God’s covenant with Abraham stands, as does the spiritual bond between God and Israel, Abraham’s descendants.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 105:6-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`)

## Psalms 105:8 (id: 23758)

The covenant is the Lord’s unbreakable promise to be the Father of Abraham and his descendants (see [Genesis 12:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:9); [15:1–21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:1-Gen15:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 105:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Psalms 105:11 (id: 23743)

God promised the land of Canaan to Abraham's descendants as their special possession. They could live there as God's people ([Psalm 105:44–45](https://ref.ly/Ps105:44-Ps105:45); [Genesis 12:7](https://ref.ly/Gen12:7); [15:7–21](https://ref.ly/Gen15:7-Gen15:21)).

## Psalms 106:1 (id: 23764)

*Hallelujah*! is a Hebrew word (*haleluyah*) that means “Praise the Lord.” This phrase appears in four small groups in the psalms:

* [Psalm 111–113](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1-Ps113:9)
* [Psalm 115–117](https://ref.ly/Ps115:1-Ps117:2)

## Psalms 106:1–48 (id: 23759)

* The Israelite community in exile confessed the Lord’s goodness and justice and their own past sins. From their humble state in exile, they asked for redemption and restoration. They hoped to be gathered back from all the places the Lord had scattered them in his anger.

    The psalm ends with a memory of the Lord’s past mercy and hope for renewed mercy ([Psalm 106:44–48](https://ref.ly/Ps106:44-Ps106:48)). Israel failed, but the Lord remains constant.

* Verses 1, [47–48](https://ref.ly/Ps47:1-Ps48:14) (along with [Psalms 96](https://ref.ly/Ps96:1-Ps96:13); [105:1–15](https://ref.ly/Ps105:1-Ps105:15)) are included in David’s song of praise recorded in [1 Chronicles 16:1–43](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:1-1Chr16:43).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 106:1-48

## Psalms 106:30–33 (id: 23771)

Phinehas killed an Israelite man who openly turned away from the Lord ([Numbers 25:3–8](https://ref.ly/Num25:3-Num25:8)). Because his action stopped the plague, the psalm credits him with righteousness. In this psalm he is presented as a stronger example of zeal for the Lord than Moses, who failed at Meribah ([Numbers 20:11–13](https://ref.ly/Num20:11-Num20:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 106:30-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Phinehas (ID: `person:Phinehas`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Meribah (ID: `place:Meribah.2`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`)

## Psalms 106:34 (id: 23772)

Israel continued to disobey the word of the Lord while living in the promised land. Instead of destroying the nations, Israel accepted them and their customs (see [Judges 2:1–3](https://ref.ly/Judg2:1-Judg2:3), [11–15](https://ref.ly/Judg2:11-Judg2:15); [3:5–6](https://ref.ly/Judg3:5-Judg3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 106:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 106:48 (id: 23782)

* This doxology (a short song of praise) ends Book Four (compare [Psalms 41:13](https://ref.ly/Ps41:13); [89:52](https://ref.ly/Ps89:52)).
* *from everlasting to everlasting*: The reference to God’s eternal nature links to the opening of Book Four ([Psalm 90:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps90:1-Ps90:2)).
* *Let all the people say, “Amen!”*: The Hebrew word *’amen* means “It is true.” The faithful accept the Lord’s will during exile and his promise to be their God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 106:48

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Psalms 107:1–43 (id: 23786)

This psalm is a hymn of praise to God the Redeemer. The psalmist begins by urging the redeemed to praise God for his goodness ([Psalm 107:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps107:1-Ps107:3)). The psalmist then presents four examples of people whom the Lord saved from trouble ([Psalm 107:4–32](https://ref.ly/Ps107:4-Ps107:32)). He summarizes the Lord’s control over everything ([Psalm 107:33–42](https://ref.ly/Ps107:33-Ps107:42)) and ends with an invitation to recognize God’s loving devotion in all parts of life ([107:43](https://ref.ly/Ps107:43)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 107:1-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Praise (ID: `keyterm:Praise`); Fool (ID: `keyterm:Fool`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 110:1 (id: 23835)

* The king of Israel had the great privilege of being the Lord’s adopted son ([Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12); [1 Chronicles 22:10](https://ref.ly/1Chr22:10)), but he failed ([Psalm 89](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1-Ps89:52)). This decree restores and elevates his position.
* *The LORD*: This title represents the Hebrew name *Yahweh,* which is God’s personal name (see [Exodus 3:6–14](https://ref.ly/Exod3:6-Exod3:14); [6:2–8](https://ref.ly/Exod6:2-Exod6:8); [20:2](https://ref.ly/Exod20:2)).
* *my Lord:* The Hebrew word *’adoni* means “my master” or “my superior.” The psalmist understood God’s messianic ruler (the king chosen by God) to be greater than himself. The New Testament often quotes this psalm to show that Jesus is the Messiah ([Matthew 22:44–45](https://ref.ly/Matt22:44-Matt22:45); [Mark 12:36–37](https://ref.ly/Mark12:36-Mark12:37); [Luke 20:42–44](https://ref.ly/Luke20:42-Luke20:44); [Acts 2:34–35](https://ref.ly/Acts2:34-Acts2:35); [Hebrews 1:13](https://ref.ly/Heb1:13)).
* The Lord’s *right hand* represents his authority, strength, presence, and benefits. The Lord chose the descendant of David and raised him up to be close to him ([Psalm 80:17](https://ref.ly/Ps80:17)).

    Jesus claimed this position for himself ([Matthew 26:64](https://ref.ly/Matt26:64); [Mark 14:62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:62); [16:19](https://ref.ly/Mark16:19); [Luke 22:69](https://ref.ly/Luke22:69)), and the apostles announced that Jesus, having gone up to heaven, sits at the right hand of the Father ([Acts 2:33](https://ref.ly/Acts2:33); [5:31](https://ref.ly/Acts5:31); [7:55–56](https://ref.ly/Acts7:55-Acts7:56); [Romans 8:34](https://ref.ly/Rom8:34); [Ephesians 1:20](https://ref.ly/Eph1:20); [Colossians 3:1](https://ref.ly/Col3:1); [Hebrews 1:3](https://ref.ly/Heb1:3); [8:1](https://ref.ly/Heb8:1); [10:12](https://ref.ly/Heb10:12); [12:2](https://ref.ly/Heb12:2)).

* Ancient Near Eastern kings were sometimes shown placing their feet on the backs of their defeated enemies as on a footstool (see [Hebrews 10:12–13](https://ref.ly/Heb10:12-Heb10:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 110:1–7 (id: 23834)

Jesus and the apostles quote this psalm to explain the unique role and authority of Jesus as the Messiah ([Matthew 22:43–45](https://ref.ly/Matt22:43-Matt22:45); [Acts 2:32–36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:32-Acts2:36)). The psalm also confirms God’s covenant with David. It gives hope that God will fulfill his promise in the future (see [2 Samuel 7:8–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8-2Sam7:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Melchizedek (ID: `person:Melchizedek`); Out of Free Will (ID: `keyterm:OutOfFreeWill`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Out of Free Will (ID: `keyterm:OutOfFreeWill`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`)

## Psalms 110:4 (id: 23837)

* God had made an unbreakable oath and covenant with David. When the exile seemed to end the line of kings from David's family ([Psalm 89:38](https://ref.ly/Ps89:38)), it created a crisis for God’s people. The placement of [Psalm 110](https://ref.ly/Ps110:1-Ps110:7) in Book Five points again to the future of David’s line. David’s descendant must live according to God’s standard of integrity ([Psalm 101](https://ref.ly/Ps101:1-Ps101:8)). His rule will also be shaped by God’s kingship ([Psalms 93–100](https://ref.ly/Ps93:1-Ps100:5)).
* *You are a priest forever*: The role of God’s messianic ruler changes. The kings from David’s family had a limited priestly role. They supported and organized the worship of the temple. For example, David brought the ark to Jerusalem ([2 Samuel 6:15](https://ref.ly/2Sam6:15)) and organized temple services ([1 Chronicles 6:31–48](https://ref.ly/1Chr6:31-1Chr6:48); [15:11–26](https://ref.ly/1Chr15:11-1Chr15:26); [16:4–42](https://ref.ly/1Chr16:4-1Chr16:42); [23:4–32](https://ref.ly/1Chr23:4-1Chr23:32); [25:1](https://ref.ly/1Chr25:1)). Solomon oversaw the construction of the temple ([1 Kings 5:1–7:51](https://ref.ly/1Kgs5:1-1Kgs7:51)).

    Even so, the offices of king and priest were separate in Israel. In the messianic kingdom, the king will also serve as a priest. He will be like Melchizedek, the king of Salem who was also a priest ([Genesis 14:18](https://ref.ly/Gen14:18)). The New Testament explains that Jesus Christ fulfills this new order of priesthood (see [Hebrews 5:6–10](https://ref.ly/Heb5:6-Heb5:10); [6:20–7:25](https://ref.ly/Heb6:20-Heb7:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Melchizedek (ID: `person:Melchizedek`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 110:5–6 (id: 23838)

* *at Your right hand*: This position brings help and protection (see [Psalms 16:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps16:7-Ps16:8); [108:13](https://ref.ly/Ps108:13)).
* The Lord will strike down rebellious kings. Rebellion against the Lord leads to death ([Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12); [Romans 6:23](https://ref.ly/Rom6:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

God’s messianic ruler wins victory with renewed strength, like a traveler who drinks from streams along the way ([Psalm 110:3](https://ref.ly/Ps110:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 110:7


## Psalms 111:1–5 (id: 23843)

The godly praise God and delight themselves in his great works. His mighty acts reflect his righteousness, grace, and compassion (see [Psalm 116:5–7](https://ref.ly/Ps116:5-Ps116:7)). All who submit themselves to him experience his glory.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`)

## Psalms 111:1–10 (id: 23841)

This wisdom psalm contains many references to the works of the Lord that reveal his character. God is:

* righteous,
* gracious,
* compassionate,
* faithful,
* just,
* trustworthy,
* upright,
* holy, and
* awe\-inspiring ([Psalm 111:3](https://ref.ly/Ps111:3), [4](https://ref.ly/Ps111:4), [7](https://ref.ly/Ps111:7), [8](https://ref.ly/Ps111:8), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps111:9)).

God's character gives hope in the covenant relationship ([Psalm 111:5](https://ref.ly/Ps111:5)). God rescued Israel from Egypt ([Psalm 111:9](https://ref.ly/Ps111:9)). God gave them the land ([111:6](https://ref.ly/Ps111:6)). God provides for his people ([111:5](https://ref.ly/Ps111:5)). However, God expects those in this relationship to also be righteous and have integrity.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:1-10

## Psalms 111:5 (id: 23845)

God takes care of his people’s needs, even providing food. He remembers his covenant with Abraham (see [Psalm 105:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps105:8-Ps105:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 111:6–8 (id: 23846)

Giving Israel the land of other nations was just and good. The Lord was judging those nations and showing grace to Israel. However, if the Israelites do not live faithfully and with integrity, they will also lose the land. The Lord’s people must reflect his character.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`)

## Psalms 111:9 (id: 23847)

The people of the exodus generation went free because of God’s grace.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Psalms 111:10 (id: 23844)

True wisdom is the source of life ([Proverbs 3:18](https://ref.ly/Prov3:18); [9:11](https://ref.ly/Prov9:11); [Ecclesiastes 7:12](https://ref.ly/Eccl7:12)). It starts with the fear of the Lord ([Proverbs 9:10](https://ref.ly/Prov9:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 111:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Insight (ID: `keyterm:Insight`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 112:1–5 (id: 23849)

The fear of the Lord leads to a wise life that honors God and brings blessing to the family and the community. The godly follow God with joy. Their obedience does not come from fear like a slave obeying a master.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 112:1–10 (id: 23848)

This psalm repeats the themes of wisdom and the fear of the Lord ([Psalm 111](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1-Ps111:10)). Wise people have reasons to be happy ([Psalm 112:1–5](https://ref.ly/Ps112:1-Ps112:5)) and strong ([112:6–10](https://ref.ly/Ps112:6-Ps112:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Horn (ID: `keyterm:Horn`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 112:2–3 (id: 23851)

*blessed*: Everyone will recognize the children of godly people (see [Psalm 127:4](https://ref.ly/Ps127:4)) because the godly leave a legacy for their children (see [1:3](https://ref.ly/Ps1:3); compare [Matthew 6:20](https://ref.ly/Matt6:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 112:4–5 (id: 23852)

Godly people imitate God by being generous, compassionate, and righteous ([Psalms 51:1](https://ref.ly/Ps51:1); [111:1–10](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1-Ps111:10); [2 Corinthians 9:9](https://ref.ly/2Cor9:9)). They lend money to the poor without charging interest ([Psalms 15:5](https://ref.ly/Ps15:5); [112:9](https://ref.ly/Ps112:9); [Exodus 22:25](https://ref.ly/Exod22:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`)

## Psalms 112:6 (id: 23853)

The wise avoid mistakes because they are honest and compassionate ([Psalms 15:5](https://ref.ly/Ps15:5); [37:23–24](https://ref.ly/Ps37:23-Ps37:24)). People will remember them for a long time ([Proverbs 10:7](https://ref.ly/Prov10:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:6


## Psalms 112:8 (id: 23854)

Because the godly trust that God will rescue them and give them victory, they face their enemies with confidence ([Psalms 59:10](https://ref.ly/Ps59:10); [112:4](https://ref.ly/Ps112:4); [118:7](https://ref.ly/Ps118:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 112:9 (id: 23855)

Paul quotes this verse in his instructions about generous giving ([2 Corinthians 9:9](https://ref.ly/2Cor9:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Horn (ID: `keyterm:Horn`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Psalms 112:10 (id: 23850)

* The wicked *gnash their teeth* in anger due to their bitter envy ([Psalms 35:16](https://ref.ly/Ps35:16); [37:12](https://ref.ly/Ps37:12)). The strength and reputation of the godly create conflict with those who wish them harm.
* The wicked will retreat in fear and shame.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 112:10


## Psalms 113:1–3 (id: 23857)

*the name of the LORD*: God's personal name (Hebrew *Yahweh;* see [Exodus 3:15](https://ref.ly/Exod3:15)) reveals who he is (his nature, character, and reputation). He is exalted at all times (now and forever) and in every place (from east to west).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 113:1–9 (id: 23858)

This song of praise celebrates the greatness of the Lord's name and glory. God is exalted and glorious in heaven ([Psalm 113:4](https://ref.ly/Ps113:4)). He also stoops down to care for the lowly and needy on earth ([Psalm 113:5–9](https://ref.ly/Ps113:5-Ps113:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 113:1–118.29 (id: 23856)

These psalms are called the Egyptian Hallel. *Hallel* means “praise.” Jewish people use these psalms during Passover, which began when God brought Israel out of Egypt in the exodus. The people recite [Psalms 113–114](https://ref.ly/Ps113:1-Ps114:8) before the Passover meal, and [Psalms 115–118](https://ref.ly/Ps115:1-Ps118:29) are recited afterward.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:1-118:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Simple Person (ID: `keyterm:SimplePerson`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Praise (ID: `keyterm:Praise`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Psalms 113:4 (id: 23859)

The Lord’s glory extends to the heavens ([Psalms 57:5](https://ref.ly/Ps57:5), [11](https://ref.ly/Ps57:11); [99:2](https://ref.ly/Ps99:2); [108:5](https://ref.ly/Ps108:5); [138:6](https://ref.ly/Ps138:6); [Isaiah 6:1](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1); [57:15](https://ref.ly/Isa57:15)) and throughout the world of human relationships.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 113:5–9 (id: 23860)

The Lord is unlike anyone else, yet he personally cares for people on earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:5-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 113:6 (id: 23861)

The Lord humbles himself to connect directly with his people (see [Psalm 138:6](https://ref.ly/Ps138:6); [Philippians 2:6–8](https://ref.ly/Phil2:6-Phil2:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:6


## Psalms 113:7–8 (id: 23862)

These phrases are a quotation from Hannah’s prayer of praise ([1 Samuel 2:8–9a](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:8-1Sam2:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:7-8


## Psalms 113:9 (id: 23863)

* People in this culture often treated a woman without children with shame or contempt ([Genesis 16:4](https://ref.ly/Gen16:4)). But the Lord has the power to make her a happy mother. For example:

    + Sarah ([Genesis 21:2](https://ref.ly/Gen21:2)),
        + Rebekah ([Genesis 25:21](https://ref.ly/Gen25:21)),
        + Rachel ([Genesis 30:23](https://ref.ly/Gen30:23)), and
        + Hannah ([1 Samuel 1:20](https://ref.ly/1Sam1:20)).
* God's greatness and goodness give people many reasons to praise him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 113:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 114:1–2 (id: 23864)

* At the time of Israel’s exodus from Egypt and their entrance into the promised land, Israel was one holy kingdom ([Exodus 19:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod19:5-Exod19:6)).
* The mention of Judah and Israel as separate lands shows the psalm was written after the kingdom was divided ([1 Kings 12:16–17](https://ref.ly/1Kgs12:16-1Kgs12:17); compare [1 Samuel 11:8](https://ref.ly/1Sam11:8); [1 Kings 1:35](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:35)), and probably after the exile. Judah became God's sanctuary because the people worshiped God there. His tabernacle, and later his temple, were located there.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 114:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Psalms 114:1–8 (id: 23865)

This song celebrates the Lord's power in nature. It remembers Israel's start as a nation during the exodus ([Psalm 114:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps114:1-Ps114:2)) and their entry into the promised land ([114:3–6](https://ref.ly/Ps114:3-Ps114:6)). It encourages respect for the God of Jacob ([Psalm 114:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps114:7-Ps114:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 114:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`)

## Psalms 114:3–4 (id: 23866)

* *fled*: God parted the waters so that Israel could escape Egypt ([Psalm 77:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps77:17-Ps77:18); [Exodus 14](https://ref.ly/Exod14:1-Exod14:31)).
* The Jordan River *turned back* when Israel arrived to cross it (see [Joshua 3](https://ref.ly/Josh3:1-Josh3:17)).
* *skipped like rams*: God's presence made the mountains quake ([Exodus 19:16–20](https://ref.ly/Exod19:16-Exod19:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 114:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 114:8 (id: 23867)

The Lord miraculously provided for his people in hard times after the exile, just as he had done in the wilderness after the exodus.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 114:8


## Psalms 115:1–3 (id: 23869)

The community confesses their faith that God will rescue them for the glory of his name.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 115:1–18 (id: 23868)

This is a song of thanksgiving for God's blessings. The community gives all glory to the Lord, the true source of hope and blessing. Those who trust in idols feel great disappointment. The Creator's blessing extends to future generations ([Psalm 115:14](https://ref.ly/Ps115:14)), especially to the priests ([115:12](https://ref.ly/Ps115:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Psalms 115:2–3 (id: 23873)

*Where is their God?*: People question God's presence or power when he does not act as they expect (compare [Psalms 14:1](https://ref.ly/Ps14:1); [42:3](https://ref.ly/Ps42:3), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps42:10)). However, God is in the heavens. He has all power, authority, and knowledge. He does as he wishes, not what people think he should do. He carries out his plans according to his will ([Ephesians 1:11](https://ref.ly/Eph1:11)) and acts in his own time and ways.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`)

## Psalms 115:4–11 (id: 23874)

* This entire section is repeated in [Psalm 135:15–20](https://ref.ly/Ps135:15-Ps135:20).
* Unlike the Lord ([Psalm 95:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps95:3-Ps95:5)), idols cannot do anything. They are useless.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:4-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Psalms 115:8 (id: 23875)

Worshiping idols leads one astray and corrupts those who trust in them ([Isaiah 44:20](https://ref.ly/Isa44:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`)

## Psalms 115:9–11 (id: 23876)

*Israel … O house of Aaron … You who fear the LORD*: The psalmist urges everyone in the God\-fearing community to trust in the living God. This threefold address assures them that the Lord is their true helper and shield (compare [Psalm 118:2–4](https://ref.ly/Ps118:2-Ps118:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`)

## Psalms 115:12–13 (id: 23870)

The groups mentioned in [Psalm 115:9–11](https://ref.ly/Ps115:9-Ps115:11) receive assurance of the Lord's blessing, no matter their social status (see [113:8](https://ref.ly/Ps113:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`)

## Psalms 115:14–15 (id: 23871)

* The psalmist blesses ([Psalm 107:37–38](https://ref.ly/Ps107:37-Ps107:38), [41](https://ref.ly/Ps107:41)) all who trust in and fear the Lord. The Creator will care for the families of those who fear him.
* *the Maker of heaven and earth*: This phrase speaks about how the Lord created everything and has power over all creation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 115:16–18 (id: 23872)

* *The highest heavens belong to the LORD*: God's exaltation starts in heaven ([Psalm 113:4](https://ref.ly/Ps113:4)), far above the gods of other nations. The earth also belongs to him ([Psalm 47:9](https://ref.ly/Ps47:9); [95:4](https://ref.ly/Ps95:4)).
* The dead cannot participate in God’s blessings. The living praise God in thanksgiving for his blessings ([Psalm 79:13](https://ref.ly/Ps79:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 115:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 116:1 (id: 23882)

*I love the LORD*: This unique opening expresses affection for and commitment to the Lord (see [Psalm 18:1](https://ref.ly/Ps18:1); [Deuteronomy 6:5](https://ref.ly/Deut6:5)) on the basis of what the Lord has done.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 116:1–4 (id: 23878)

The psalmist praises the Lord for answering his prayers and rescuing him from death.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 116:1–19 (id: 23877)

This song of thanksgiving rejoices in God’s character and in rescue from death ([Psalm 116:1–11](https://ref.ly/Ps116:1-Ps116:11)). It gives an assurance of God’s protection in life and reminds the godly that the Lord watches over them even at death. The psalmist commits to living as God’s servant and resolves to honor him publicly ([Psalm 116:12–19](https://ref.ly/Ps116:12-Ps116:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:1-19

## Psalms 116:2 (id: 23883)

Because the Lord responds to the cry of his people([Psalm 107:41](https://ref.ly/Ps107:41); [113:6–8](https://ref.ly/Ps113:6-Ps113:8)), the psalmist now strongly believes in prayer.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:2


## Psalms 116:3 (id: 23884)

It is not clear if the psalmist’s crisis was a literal near\-death experience or if he was using the term figuratively.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 116:4 (id: 23885)

This is the first of three times that the psalmist calls on the Lord’s name (which represents God's reputation).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 116:5–7 (id: 23886)

The gracious, righteous, and compassionate Lord (see [Psalm 111:1–5](https://ref.ly/Ps111:1-Ps111:5)) rescued and gave rest to the psalmist.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Simple Person (ID: `keyterm:SimplePerson`)

## Psalms 116:6 (id: 23887)

People with childlike faith readily receive God's wisdom and instruction ([Psalm 19:7–11](https://ref.ly/Ps19:7-Ps19:11); [Matthew 18:3–4](https://ref.ly/Matt18:3-Matt18:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simple Person (ID: `keyterm:SimplePerson`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 116:8–11 (id: 23888)

The psalmist’s near\-death crisis made him more aware of life and of walking before the Lord. Though he had suffered, the Lord was faithful. However, people are all liars.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 116:9 (id: 23889)

*I will walk before the LORD*: The psalmist lived with integrity. After experiencing deep distress, he now enjoys open fellowship with the Lord (see [Psalm 56:13](https://ref.ly/Ps56:13); compare [Job 42:5](https://ref.ly/Job42:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 116:10 (id: 23879)

*I believed*: Paul quotes this phrase in [2 Corinthians 4:13](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:13).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`)

## Psalms 116:12–14 (id: 23880)

* The Lord’s gracious gifts are so great that the psalmist can do nothing to repay his debt. Keeping his promises publicly demonstrates his gratitude.
* All he has done for the psalmist includes answers to prayer, rescue, and life.
* The *cup of salvation* overflows with God’s blessings ([Psalm 23:5](https://ref.ly/Ps23:5)), unlike the cup of judgment ([75:8](https://ref.ly/Ps75:8)).
* The psalmist makes *vows* ([Psalm 116:18](https://ref.ly/Ps116:18)) to praise the Lord publicly ([116:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps116:17-Ps116:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:12-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 116:15–19 (id: 23881)

No one lives or dies outside of God’s will. Having been given a second chance at life, the poet readily commits himself to the Lord with renewed acts of public devotion.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 116:15-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Praise (ID: `keyterm:Praise`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 117:1 (id: 23891)

Paul quotes this verse to show that Gentiles would eventually praise the God of Israel ([Romans 15:11](https://ref.ly/Rom15:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 117:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 117:1–2 (id: 23890)

This psalm is the shortest in the Psalter. It invites all people of the earth to praise the Lord for his *loving devotion and faithfulness.*

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 117:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 118:1–29 (id: 23892)

The psalmist joyfully thanks the Lord for his goodness and urges others to trust in his *loving devotion* ([Psalm 118:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps118:1-Ps118:4), [29](https://ref.ly/Ps118:29)). This poem is the last of the Egyptian Hallel ([Psalms 113–118](https://ref.ly/Ps113:1-Ps118:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:1-29

## Psalms 118:2–4 (id: 23896)

*Israel … the house of Aaron … those who fear the LORD*: These three groups represent the entire covenant community (compare [Psalms 115:9–11](https://ref.ly/Ps115:9-Ps115:11); [135:19–20](https://ref.ly/Ps135:19-Ps135:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:2-4

## Psalms 118:5–9 (id: 23902)

The Lord alone is the psalmist’s helper.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:5-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 118:6–7 (id: 23903)

* *The LORD is on my side*: Compare [Romans 8:31;](https://ref.ly/Rom8:31) [Hebrews 13:6](https://ref.ly/Heb13:6).
* God's presence removes the need to fear people ([Psalm 56:11](https://ref.ly/Ps56:11)). The psalmist joyfully expects God’s rescue ([Psalm 59:10](https://ref.ly/Ps59:10); [Psalm 112:8](https://ref.ly/Ps112:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 118:8–9 (id: 23904)

Trusting in the Lord is better than placing confidence in even the most powerful human leaders ([Psalms 118:6](https://ref.ly/Ps118:6); [146:3](https://ref.ly/Ps146:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 118:10–12 (id: 23893)

* Strength comes from *the name of the LORD*. This is shown by the threefold repetition of the attackers, the *name of the LORD*, and the victory he gives.
* Even when hostile people surrounded the psalmist, the Lord *extinguished them like burning thorns* (see [Isaiah 9:18](https://ref.ly/Isa9:18); [10:17](https://ref.ly/Isa10:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:10-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hornet (ID: `fauna:Hornet`); Bee (ID: `fauna:Bee`)

## Psalms 118:14–18 (id: 23894)

The psalmist celebrates the victory of the Lord’s strong *right hand,* which kept him from death.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:14-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Psalms 118:17–21 (id: 23895)

God has restored the psalmist from death to life, so he praises the Lord publicly (see [Psalm 116:6](https://ref.ly/Ps116:6), [12–19](https://ref.ly/Ps116:12-Ps116:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:17-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Psalms 118:22 (id: 23898)

The *cornerstone* shapes and supports a building or arch. It is the most important of all the stones. The Lord selects and places this stone for his sanctuary ([Isaiah 28:16](https://ref.ly/Isa28:16)). 

Jesus referred to himself as this cornerstone ([Matthew 21:42](https://ref.ly/Matt21:42); [Mark 12:10–11](https://ref.ly/Mark12:10-Mark12:11); [Luke 20:17](https://ref.ly/Luke20:17); see [Acts 4:11](https://ref.ly/Acts4:11); [Ephesians 2:20](https://ref.ly/Eph2:20); [1 Peter 2:6–7](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:6-1Pet2:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:22


## Psalms 118:22–24 (id: 23897)

The psalmist sees his own salvation as the beginning of a new day in which the Lord will do great things.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:22-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 118:24 (id: 23899)

*This is the day that the LORD has made*: The Lord's victory has begun, but it will be completed on another day ([Psalm 118:25](https://ref.ly/Ps118:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 118:25–29 (id: 23900)

The psalmist ends with prayer and thanksgiving, anticipating an even greater revelation of God’s victory and rescue.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:25-29

## Psalms 118:26 (id: 23901)

* The one who comes in the name of the Lord brings the Lord’s complete victory (see [Psalm 118:10–12](https://ref.ly/Ps118:10-Ps118:12)).
* When Jesus triumphantly entered Jerusalem, the crowds shouted blessings. They used phrases from this verse ([Matthew 21:9](https://ref.ly/Matt21:9); [Mark 11:9](https://ref.ly/Mark11:9); [Luke 19:38](https://ref.ly/Luke19:38); [John 12:13](https://ref.ly/John12:13)).

    Jesus quoted this passage as he wept over Jerusalem ([Matthew 23:39](https://ref.ly/Matt23:39); [Luke 13:35](https://ref.ly/Luke13:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 118:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 119:1–4 (id: 23906)

Wise people are happy. They have integrity (see [Psalm 84:11](https://ref.ly/Ps84:11)) and follow God's guidance. They obey God's teachings and seek them with complete devotion (see [Psalm 27:7](https://ref.ly/Ps27:7); [119:10](https://ref.ly/Ps119:10), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps119:45), [94](https://ref.ly/Ps119:94), [155](https://ref.ly/Ps119:155)). They reject all evil forms. Compare [Psalm 1:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 119:1–8 (id: 23907)

*Aleph* (א) is the first letter of the Hebrew alphabet. Psalm 119 is written as an acrostic poem. Each section has eight verses, and every verse in that section begins with the same Hebrew letter. The first section begins with *Aleph*.

God, the author of these instructions*,* commands that his followers live by them. The psalmist prays to be given the grace to live by that revelation.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 119:1–176 (id: 23905)

This psalm blends wisdom, lament, praise, thanksgiving, and confession. It often uses eight Hebrew words translated as: 

* “Instructions”
* “law”
* “words”
* “commandments”
* “statutes”
* “judgments”
* “promises”
* “precepts“

These words encourage love for and obedience to God's instructions in the Scriptures (see [2 Timothy 3:14–17](https://ref.ly/2Tim3:14-2Tim3:17)). A deep commitment to these instructions unifies the psalm.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:1-176

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Simple Person (ID: `keyterm:SimplePerson`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Psalms 119:4 (id: 23944)

To keep God’s commandments means to guard them by obeying them.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:4


## Psalms 119:9 (id: 23961)

* A young person often acts foolishly ([Proverbs 22:15](https://ref.ly/Prov22:15)) and can easily make mistakes that ruin their life ([Proverbs 7:6–23](https://ref.ly/Prov7:6-Prov7:23)). However, youth is the best time to build self\-discipline and seek wisdom (see [Psalm 34:11](https://ref.ly/Ps34:11); [Proverbs 1:4](https://ref.ly/Prov1:4)).
* *keep his way pure*: This means avoiding corruption by evil and sin and developing the integrity described in [Psalm 15](https://ref.ly/Ps15:1-Ps15:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:9


## Psalms 119:9–16 (id: 23956)

*Beth* (ב) is the second letter of the Hebrew alphabet. It makes the beginning of the next section of this psalm.

The psalmist is determined to stay pure and encourages gaining wisdom through God's principles ([Psalm 119:9](https://ref.ly/Ps119:9)) and instructions ([119:10–16](https://ref.ly/Ps119:10-Ps119:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:9-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 119:10 (id: 23912)

The student asks God the teacher to discipline him so that he will not stray.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:10


## Psalms 119:14 (id: 23923)

God's wisdom is more valuable than any wealth people desire ([Psalm 119:72](https://ref.ly/Ps119:72), [127](https://ref.ly/Ps119:127); [Proverbs 3:14–15](https://ref.ly/Prov3:14-Prov3:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Precept (ID: `keyterm:Precept`)

## Psalms 119:15 (id: 23926)

The path of wisdom involves thinking deeply about God's teachings (see [Psalm 119:1](https://ref.ly/Ps119:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Ps119:3)). Wise people consider the meaning of God's word.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:15


## Psalms 119:17–18 (id: 23930)

The psalmist wants to live a life full of praise and obedience to please the Lord ([Psalms 118:17](https://ref.ly/Ps118:17); [119:25](https://ref.ly/Ps119:25), [77](https://ref.ly/Ps119:77), [116](https://ref.ly/Ps119:116), [144](https://ref.ly/Ps119:144), [175](https://ref.ly/Ps119:175)). Only the Lord can remove his enemies and help him understand the wonderful truths in God's teachings.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:17-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Psalms 119:17–24 (id: 23931)

*Gimel* (ג) is the third letter of the Hebrew alphabet. It marks the beginning of the next section of this psalm.

Like a stranger in a hostile land, God's servant needs God's guidance.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:17-24

## Psalms 119:25–32 (id: 23935)

*Daleth* (ד) is the fourth letter of the Hebrew alphabet. It marks the beginning of the next section of this psalm.

The poet feels deep pain and sadness but asks the Lord to give him strength. He wants to follow the Lord. Suffering and death may result from the Lord’s discipline ([Psalms 118:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps118:17-Ps118:18); [119:67](https://ref.ly/Ps119:67), [71](https://ref.ly/Ps119:71), [75](https://ref.ly/Ps119:75)), but his instruction renews the weary soul (see [119:12](https://ref.ly/Ps119:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:25-32

## Psalms 119:29 (id: 23936)

* *path of deceit*: Self\-deception comes from sinful human nature ([Jeremiah 17:9](https://ref.ly/Jer17:9)). It leads to foolishness.
* Access to God’s word is a privilege, not a right.

## Psalms 119:73–80 (id: 23952)

*Yodh* (י) is the tenth letter of the Hebrew alphabet. It marks the beginning of the next section of this psalm. 

In this prayer for comfort and compassion, the psalmist commits himself to his Creator, who has afflicted him. He wants God to pay back his enemies for the wrongs they have done. He also prays to receive the gift of life. He not only prays for himself but also considers the whole community of the godly, asking that God’s answers to prayer would encourage them.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 119:73-80

## Psalms 120:1–7 (id: 23969)

This short, individual lament expresses the psalmist’s trust that the Lord will deal with deceptive evildoers. It also laments living as a stranger among hateful liars. The psalmist’s picture of his life in a foreign land might have been a reality as he set out for Jerusalem, or it might depict his feelings and concerns as he begins his pilgrimage. Overwhelmed with injustice, he trusts God and looks forward to arriving in the Lord’s presence.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 120:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`); Meshech (ID: `place:Meshech`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Broom (ID: `flora:Broom`)

## Psalms 120:1–134.3 (id: 23967)

These short and memorable songs were sung by pilgrims as they traveled to Jerusalem for the national festivals held three times each year ([Deuteronomy 16:16](https://ref.ly/Deut16:16)). The title of these psalms includes a Hebrew word that can mean “steps” or “stairs.” Some scholars understand it to mean “going up to Jerusalem.” These psalms, often called the Songs of Ascents, lead the listener on a journey to Jerusalem to praise God for his goodness to Israel through history.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 120:1-134:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); David (ID: `person:David`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); Mount Hermon (ID: `place:HermonMount`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Meshech (ID: `place:Meshech`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

* The Divine Warrior (see [Psalms 7:12–13](https://ref.ly/Ps7:12-Ps7:13); [11:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps11:4-Ps11:6)) will attack the liars with sharp arrows and burning coals.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 120:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Deceit (ID: `keyterm:Deceit`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Broom (ID: `flora:Broom`)

## Psalms 120:5 (id: 23972)

Meshech was a Japhethite territory far to the north of Canaan. Kedar was an Ishmaelite territory in Arabia. Violent people lived in both places. 

Meshech was known for its slave trade ([Ezekiel 27:13](https://ref.ly/Ezek27:13); see also [Exodus 21:16](https://ref.ly/Exod21:16)), and Kedar was known for its warriors ([Isaiah 21:16–17](https://ref.ly/Isa21:16-Isa21:17)). These places represented the rebellious and violent land where the psalmist suffered.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 120:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Meshech (ID: `place:Meshech`); Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`)

## Psalms 120:5–7 (id: 23971)

The psalmist feels the pain of being alone, isolated, and in constant conflict.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Meshech (ID: `place:Meshech`); Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 120:6-7

## Psalms 121:1 (id: 23975)

The mountains were known for idol worship ([Deuteronomy 12:2](https://ref.ly/Deut12:2)), and Israel was drawn to the hilltop shrines ([Hosea 4:13](https://ref.ly/Hos4:13)). This verse can be seen as a rhetorical question expecting a "no" answer: The gods of the hilltop shrines cannot save anyone. 

Others understand the mountains to be a symbol of the Lord’s strength ([Psalm 95:4](https://ref.ly/Ps95:4)). In that case, the question would be taken literally (*From where does my help come?*), with the answer in the next verse.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 121:1

## Psalms 121:4 (id: 23977)

God watches over his own, even and especially when they face many troubles ([Psalms 12:5](https://ref.ly/Ps12:5), [7](https://ref.ly/Ps12:7); [69:29](https://ref.ly/Ps69:29); [91:14](https://ref.ly/Ps91:14); [146:9](https://ref.ly/Ps146:9)).

## Psalms 121:4–6 (id: 23976)

* God stands with Israel, offering support and care to the faithful (see [Psalm 16:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps16:7-Ps16:8)).
* Both the sun and the moon were worshiped as gods in the ancient Near East. But God limits their power. They are part of creation and also praise the Lord ([Psalm 148:3](https://ref.ly/Ps148:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 121:4-6

## Psalms 122:1–9 (id: 23978)

The psalmist praises Jerusalem ([Psalm 122:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps122:3-Ps122:5)) and prays for the city’s peace ([122:6–9](https://ref.ly/Ps122:6-Ps122:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 122:1-9
