This thanksgiving song describes what might happen if the Lord were not present with his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 124:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); David (ID: `person:David`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 124:4–5 (id: 23989)

*the raging waters*: The enemies could have defeated the Israelites and destroyed them if the Lord had not supported his people (compare [Psalm 69:15](https://ref.ly/Ps69:15); [Jeremiah 46:8](https://ref.ly/Jer46:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 124:4-5


## Psalms 124:6–8 (id: 23990)

The enemies of God's people were like lions or bears that tear prey with their teeth (see [Psalm 57:4](https://ref.ly/Ps57:4); [Proverbs 28:15](https://ref.ly/Prov28:15)). The Lord shut their mouths ([Daniel 6:22](https://ref.ly/Dan6:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 124:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`)

## Psalms 125:1–5 (id: 23991)

The psalmist expresses the community's trust in the Lord's protection ([Psalm 125:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps125:1-Ps125:2)) and prays for peace ([125:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps125:3-Ps125:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 125:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Psalms 125:2 (id: 23992)

*surrounds*: God guards and protects like a wall of fire ([Zechariah 2:5](https://ref.ly/Zech2:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 125:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 125:3 (id: 23993)

The godly may be tempted to abandon God’s ways because of pressure from others or from the surrounding culture.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 125:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Psalms 125:4–5 (id: 23994)

People who do not follow the "straight paths" are foolish ([Proverbs 2:12–15](https://ref.ly/Prov2:12-Prov2:15)). God gives his goodness to the godly (see [Psalm 122:6–9](https://ref.ly/Ps122:6-Ps122:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 125:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Psalms 126:1–3 (id: 23995)

The Lord brought about Israel’s restoration from exile, just as he had brought Israel out of Egypt ([Psalm 114:1](https://ref.ly/Ps114:1)). The restoration transformed the weeping of the Lord’s people into laughter and showed the Lord’s character to the nations ([Psalm 9:11](https://ref.ly/Ps9:11); [64:9](https://ref.ly/Ps64:9); [Isaiah 12:4](https://ref.ly/Isa12:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 126:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Psalms 126:1–6 (id: 23996)

This psalm celebrates Israel's return from exile ([Psalm 126:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps126:1-Ps126:3)). The psalmist then prays that God will fully restore the people ([Psalm 126:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps126:4-Ps126:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 126:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Sheaf (ID: `realia:Sheaf`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Psalms 126:4–6 (id: 23997)

* The prophets had spoken of the restoration as a time when the wilderness would change into the garden of Eden ([Isaiah 51:3](https://ref.ly/Isa51:3); [Ezekiel 47](https://ref.ly/Ezek47:1-Ezek47:23)). However, the reality was far from paradise. Here, the restoration community prays for complete redemption (see [Haggai 2:19](https://ref.ly/Hag2:19)).
* *like streams in the Negev*: Seasonal rains filled the dry riverbeds (*wadis*), making the land green again. The prayer seeks a similar change in Israel’s fortunes, fulfilling the imagery of the "Second Exodus" in Isaiah (see "The Promise of a Second Exodus" Theme Note).
* *carrying sheaves of grain*: This harvest represents God's complete restoration.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 126:4-6
* **Associated Articles:** The Promise of a Second Exodus (ID: `500298@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sheaf (ID: `realia:Sheaf`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Psalms 127:1–2 (id: 23998)

* *Unless*: These conditional statements emphasize that the Lord is the one who protects the city (compare [Psalm 124:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps124:1-Ps124:2)).
* Human effort achieves nothing unless the Lord blesses it (see [Psalm 44:3](https://ref.ly/Ps44:3)).
* *you rise early and stay up late*: This phrase describes people who work constantly and live with pressure and competition.
* Those whom God loves can rest and sleep in peace ([Psalm 3:5](https://ref.ly/Ps3:5); [4:8](https://ref.ly/Ps4:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 127:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Psalms 127:1–5 (id: 23999)

Blessings and protection come from God. Without his presence, success is not possible ([Psalm 127:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps127:1-Ps127:2)). This is especially true for children, who are gifts from God ([Psalm 127:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ps127:3-Ps127:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 127:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Solomon (ID: `person:Solomon`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Quiver (ID: `realia:Quiver`)

## Psalms 127:3–5 (id: 24000)

Children are one of God’s blessings. In Hebrew, the word for children (*banim*) sounds like the word for builders (*bonim*; [Psalm 127:1](https://ref.ly/Ps127:1)). This wordplay shows that having children helps build a family.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 127:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Quiver (ID: `realia:Quiver`)

## Psalms 127:4–5 (id: 24001)

*Like arrows*: The image compares children to arrows in a warrior’s hand. Children strengthen and support the family and help defend its honor when others accuse or oppose them (compare [Psalms 120:4](https://ref.ly/Ps120:4); [7:12–13](https://ref.ly/Ps7:12-Ps7:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 127:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Quiver (ID: `realia:Quiver`)

## Psalms 128:1–2 (id: 24002)

*eating* *the fruit of your labor*: This means gaining benefit from whatever work one does.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 128:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 128:1–6 (id: 24003)

The godly, who fear the Lord, will know true happiness ([Psalm 128:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps128:1-Ps128:2)). The Lord is with them (see [Psalm 127:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps127:1-Ps127:2)), strengthens their family life ([128:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps128:3-Ps128:4)), and blesses them for generations ([128:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps128:5-Ps128:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 128:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Psalms 128:3 (id: 24004)

Children are like strong young olive trees because they are full of potential. Olive oil symbolizes God's blessing ([Psalm 104:15](https://ref.ly/Ps104:15); [Numbers 18:12](https://ref.ly/Num18:12); [Jeremiah 31:12](https://ref.ly/Jer31:12); [Haggai 2:19](https://ref.ly/Hag2:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 128:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Olive Tree (ID: `flora:OliveTree`)

## Psalms 128:4–6 (id: 24005)

* God will continually bless those who fear him ([Psalm 133:3](https://ref.ly/Ps133:3)) from his dwelling in Zion ([20:2](https://ref.ly/Ps20:2); [50:2](https://ref.ly/Ps50:2); [110:2](https://ref.ly/Ps110:2)), no matter where they live.
* *children's children*: God's blessings continue through generations for those who are faithful ([Psalms 79:12–13](https://ref.ly/Ps79:12-Ps79:13); [89:1](https://ref.ly/Ps89:1); [100:4–5](https://ref.ly/Ps100:4-Ps100:5); [103:17–19](https://ref.ly/Ps103:17-Ps103:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 128:4-6

## Psalms 129:1–2 (id: 24006)

Israel’s earliest years as a nation took place while the people were living in Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 129:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Psalms 129:1–8 (id: 24007)

The psalmist briefly reviews Israel’s history of trouble ([Psalm 129:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps129:1-Ps129:2)) and redemption ([129:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps129:3-Ps129:4)). He ends with curses against the enemies and blessings on the godly ([Psalm 129:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps129:5-Ps129:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 129:1-8

## Psalms 129:3–4 (id: 24008)

*The LORD is righteous*: He rescued Israel from Egypt, from many enemy attacks, and from the Babylonian exile.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 129:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`)

## Psalms 129:6–8 (id: 24009)

Grass on a rooftop had shallow roots and would scorch easily. A crop was ignored by the harvester when the yield was low. The enemies who had sown hurt among God’s people would gain little.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 129:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sheaf (ID: `realia:Sheaf`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 130:1–3 (id: 24016)

A cry from the depths comes from a place of deep distress (compare [Psalm 69:2](https://ref.ly/Ps69:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Ps69:14); [Isaiah 51:10](https://ref.ly/Isa51:10); [Ezekiel 27:34](https://ref.ly/Ezek27:34)). Feeling weak, the psalmist prays for God's favor.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Psalms 130:1–8 (id: 24017)

Beginning with himself as a representative of the community, the psalmist cries for God’s mercy ([Psalm 130:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps130:1-Ps130:3)) out of deep distress. He places his hope in the Lord ([Psalm 130:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps130:4-Ps130:6)), trusting that the Lord will rescue in his own time and way. With this confidence, the psalmist invites Israel to wait for the Lord ([Psalm 130:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ps130:7-Ps130:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:1-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 130:3 (id: 24018)

The psalmist knows that his sins make him guilty and deserving of God’s judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 130:4–6 (id: 24019)

The good news comes in God’s readiness to forgive sinners. His grace encourages people to fear him and hope in him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 130:5 (id: 24020)

God's word gives hope ([Psalms 119:42–43](https://ref.ly/Ps119:42-Ps119:43), [49](https://ref.ly/Ps119:49); [147:11](https://ref.ly/Ps147:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 130:7–8 (id: 24021)

Israel is encouraged to hope in the Lord ([Psalms 9:18](https://ref.ly/Ps9:18); [27:14](https://ref.ly/Ps27:14); [131:3](https://ref.ly/Ps131:3)) because the Lord pays the high price for redemption and removes the sin that separates humans from him (see also [Psalm 111:9](https://ref.ly/Ps111:9); [Exodus 6:6–8](https://ref.ly/Exod6:6-Exod6:8); [2 Samuel 7:23–24](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:23-2Sam7:24); [Isaiah 43:1–4](https://ref.ly/Isa43:1-Isa43:4); [Titus 2:14](https://ref.ly/Titus2:14); [Hebrews 9:12](https://ref.ly/Heb9:12); [1 Peter 1:18](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 130:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 131:1 (id: 24023)

* The *heart* represents the entire person (see [Psalm 27:8](https://ref.ly/Ps27:8)).
* God hates arrogance, which is a foolish way of life ([Proverbs 6:16–19](https://ref.ly/Prov6:16-Prov6:19)).
* The psalmist does not trouble himself with matters that properly belong to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 131:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 131:1–3 (id: 24022)

Out of his contentment in the Lord, the psalmist invites Israel to seek their rest in God ([Psalm 131:3](https://ref.ly/Ps131:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 131:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); David (ID: `person:David`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Psalms 131:2 (id: 24024)

* The psalmist has calmed and quieted himself. He has given up his ambitions and pride ([Galatians 2:20](https://ref.ly/Gal2:20)).
* *like a weaned child*: The psalmist compares himself to a contented child who rests quietly with its mother.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 131:2


## Psalms 132:1–18 (id: 24025)

This psalm celebrates the Lord’s faithfulness to David. When David wanted to bring the ark to Jerusalem ([Psalm 132:1–5](https://ref.ly/Ps132:1-Ps132:5)), the Lord agreed and made Jerusalem his earthly home ([132:6–10](https://ref.ly/Ps132:6-Ps132:10)). God’s choice of Jerusalem also shows his choice: 

* of David's family line ([Psalm 132:11–13](https://ref.ly/Ps132:11-Ps132:13)),
* of Zion ([Psalm 132:14–17](https://ref.ly/Ps132:14-Ps132:17)), and
* of the priesthood ([Psalm 132:9](https://ref.ly/Ps132:9), [16](https://ref.ly/Ps132:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); David (ID: `person:David`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Kiriath-jearim (ID: `place:Kiriath-jearim`); Horn (ID: `keyterm:Horn`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Lamp (ID: `realia:Lamp`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)

## Psalms 132:6 (id: 24031)

* *Ephrathah* is another name for Bethlehem ([Genesis 35:19](https://ref.ly/Gen35:19); [48:7](https://ref.ly/Gen48:7)).
* *Jaar* is the singular form of the plural *Jearim*. The ark was at Kiriath\-jearim for 20 years in Samuel’s time ([1 Samuel 7:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Sam7:1-1Sam7:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Kiriath-jearim (ID: `place:Kiriath-jearim`)

## Psalms 132:6–10 (id: 24030)

These verses recall David moving the ark of the covenant to Jerusalem ([2 Samuel 6:1–15](https://ref.ly/2Sam6:1-2Sam6:15)) and the Lord's promise to David ([2 Samuel 7:4–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:4-2Sam7:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:6-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); David (ID: `person:David`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Kiriath-jearim (ID: `place:Kiriath-jearim`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)

## Psalms 132:8–10 (id: 24032)

These verses are a quotation from Solomon’s prayer at the dedication of the temple ([2 Chronicles 6:41–42](https://ref.ly/2Chr6:41-2Chr6:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:8-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); David (ID: `person:David`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)

## Psalms 132:9 (id: 24033)

The priests were to show righteousness by the way they live ([Psalm 132:16](https://ref.ly/Ps132:16); [Malachi 2:6](https://ref.ly/Mal2:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`)

## Psalms 132:11–12 (id: 24026)

*The LORD swore an oath to David* ([2 Samuel 7:4–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:4-2Sam7:16)): God's promise is unconditional, but the covenant depends on conditions (*If … then*). Those who break God's covenant should expect God's judgment as discipline (see [Psalm 89:30–37](https://ref.ly/Ps89:30-Ps89:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); David (ID: `person:David`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Psalms 132:13–17 (id: 24027)

The psalmist remembers how God chose Jerusalem and appointed the priests.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 132:13-17

## Psalms 133:1 (id: 24036)

Brothers are connected by family, by work, or by loyalty. In this psalm, *brothers* means all members of the community, including men and women, who live in unity.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 133:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Psalms 133:1–2 (id: 24034)

The psalmist speaks about the joy of relationships dedicated to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 133:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Psalms 133:1–3 (id: 24035)

In this wisdom psalm (see [Psalm 1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1-Ps1:6)), peaceful and harmonious relationships show that God is present.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 133:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mount Hermon (ID: `place:HermonMount`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Psalms 133:2 (id: 24037)

* The *fine oil* was used to appoint and set apart Aaron for his role as priest (see [Exodus 29:7](https://ref.ly/Exod29:7)).
* *head … beard … collar of his robes*: The oil set Aaron apart completely for God’s service.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 133:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Psalms 133:3 (id: 24038)

* The *dew* represents the richness of God’s blessings (his goodness and care). In Jerusalem, this moisture comes from winds off the Mediterranean Sea. Mount Hermon, which has snow on its peak, is known for its abundant moisture.
* A *blessing* is God’s life\-giving power. It shows his provision (care for needs) and protection ([Psalm 5:12](https://ref.ly/Ps5:12); [41:2](https://ref.ly/Ps41:2)). It also comes from his presence ([21:6](https://ref.ly/Ps21:6); [67:1](https://ref.ly/Ps67:1)). In the end, all nations will receive God’s blessing ([Psalms 67](https://ref.ly/Ps67:1-Ps67:7); [72:17](https://ref.ly/Ps72:17)). Zion is the central place of God's blessing ([Psalms 134:3](https://ref.ly/Ps134:3); [147:13](https://ref.ly/Ps147:13)).
* God gives everlasting life to people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 133:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mount Hermon (ID: `place:HermonMount`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 136:1–26 (id: 24052)

This is the last of the Great Hallel psalms ([Psalms 120–136](https://ref.ly/Ps120:1-Ps136:26)). It uniquely repeats the phrase *His loving devotion endures forever* in every verse. 

The hymn begins and ends with a call to praise ([Psalm 136:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps136:1-Ps136:3), [26](https://ref.ly/Ps136:26)). It declares the Lord as the Creator of everything and the Redeemer of Israel, both in ancient times ([Psalm 136:10–22](https://ref.ly/Ps136:10-Ps136:22)) and in the recent past ([136:23–24](https://ref.ly/Ps136:23-Ps136:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 136:1-26

## Psalms 136:26 (id: 24057)

The phrase *God of heaven* is a name for the Lord used during and after the exile (when many Israelites were taken from their land). It appears in:

* Ezra ([Ezra 1:2](https://ref.ly/Ezra1:2); [5:11](https://ref.ly/Ezra5:11), [12](https://ref.ly/Ezra5:12); [6:9](https://ref.ly/Ezra6:9), [10](https://ref.ly/Ezra6:10); [7:12](https://ref.ly/Ezra7:12)),

* Nehemiah ([Nehemiah 1:5](https://ref.ly/Neh1:5); [2:4](https://ref.ly/Neh2:4), [20](https://ref.ly/Neh2:20)), and
* Daniel ([Daniel 2:18](https://ref.ly/Dan2:18), [19](https://ref.ly/Dan2:19), [37](https://ref.ly/Dan2:37), [44](https://ref.ly/Dan2:44)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 136:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 137:1–9 (id: 24058)

In response to the pain of exile ([Psalm 137:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps137:1-Ps137:4)), the psalmist decides to remember Jerusalem, even though the memories cause him pain ([137:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps137:4-Ps137:6)). He also speaks a strong prayer asking God to bring justice on his enemies ([Psalm 137:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps137:7-Ps137:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 137:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Edomites (ID: `group:Edom`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Poplar (ID: `flora:Poplar`); Willow (ID: `flora:Willow`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)

## Psalms 137:2–4 (id: 24059)

Because the music of Jerusalem was connected to the joy of life in the promised land, the exile took away any reason to sing joyful songs. The Babylonians asked the Judeans to sing and dance for them, but the people were so full of grief that they could not do it.

The songs about the Lord celebrate his power and goodness, but his people were now filled with doubt and questions. They hung their harps in the trees, which may show that their joy had come to an end under God’s judgment ([Deuteronomy 21:23](https://ref.ly/Deut21:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 137:2-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Poplar (ID: `flora:Poplar`); Willow (ID: `flora:Willow`)

## Psalms 137:5–6 (id: 24060)

The psalmist pledges his loyalty to Jerusalem*,* the city at the center of God’s reign and the location of fellowship with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 137:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Psalms 137:7–9 (id: 24061)

* The poet saw how the Edomites betrayed the people of Judah, so he calls on God to judge Edom. The long conflict between Edom and Judah reached its worst point near the end of Judah’s history. At that time, the Edomites handed the Judeans over to the Babylonians ([Obadiah 1:10–11](https://ref.ly/Obad1:10-Obad1:11)) and celebrated Jerusalem's fall.
* This prayer asking God to bring justice uses strong language and can be shocking.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 137:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Edomites (ID: `group:Edom`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)

The psalmist trusts in the Lord's perfect plans and prays for God's continued involvement. The psalmist sees himself as part of God’s plans (see [Psalms 9:10](https://ref.ly/Ps9:10); [139:14](https://ref.ly/Ps139:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 138:8

## Psalms 139:1–6 (id: 24070)

The Lord knows the psalmist well. God has examined him closely, in a thorough examination that can be painful at times ([Job 7:17–19](https://ref.ly/Job7:17-Job7:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); David (ID: `person:David`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 139:1–24 (id: 24069)

In this wisdom psalm, the psalmist opens every part of his life to God. He invites God to examine his thoughts, actions, and words. Even though life is difficult, he wants to grow in wisdom so that he does not share in the judgment of the wicked ([Psalm 141:5](https://ref.ly/Ps141:5); [142:3](https://ref.ly/Ps142:3); [143:8](https://ref.ly/Ps143:8), [10](https://ref.ly/Ps143:10); [144:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps144:3-Ps144:4)). 

The psalmist also knows that he cannot escape from the Lord. His stress fades as he remembers that God has cared for him with love, even before he was born ([Psalm 139:13–18](https://ref.ly/Ps139:13-Ps139:18)). He ends by asking God to keep examining him and to guide him on the path of wisdom ([Psalm 139:23–24](https://ref.ly/Ps139:23-Ps139:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:1-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); David (ID: `person:David`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Psalms 139:2 (id: 24077)

The phrase *when I sit and when I rise* refers to everyday activities (compare [Deuteronomy 6:6–9](https://ref.ly/Deut6:6-Deut6:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Psalms 139:3–6 (id: 24078)

The Lord's all\-knowing and all\-powerful nature provides comfort to the godly, but it troubles the wicked ([Psalm 2:3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:3-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Psalms 139:7–12 (id: 24080)

The psalmist shows wonder at God being everywhere at all times.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:7-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Psalms 139:8 (id: 24081)

*if I make my bed in Sheol*: God has complete access to all he has created, even the most hidden or hard\-to\-reach places.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Psalms 139:9 (id: 24082)

* *rise on the wings of the dawn*: This poetic expression describes traveling eastward, as far as the sunrise, if that were possible.
* *the farthest sea*: This expression takes the traveler in the opposite direction, far to the west.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:9


## Psalms 139:13 (id: 24071)

* *my inmost being*: The delicate inner parts of the body include a person's very being.
* Even when hidden in his mother’s womb*,* the psalmist was guided by the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:13


## Psalms 139:17 (id: 24072)

*How precious to me are Your thoughts*: God’s careful examination shows his loving care. Life with the Lord brings blessings that are beyond words.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 139:19–24 (id: 24073)

The psalmist’s sense of awe leads him to consider the wicked and to reflect on himself. The Lord, as the examiner of all people, judges the wicked for their destructive acts and speech. So, the psalmist prays that he will not be among them.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:19-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plan (ID: `keyterm:Plan`)

## Psalms 139:21 (id: 24074)

*Do I note hate those who hate You*: Rejecting evil shows wisdom (see [Psalms 1:1](https://ref.ly/Ps1:1); [5:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps5:4-Ps5:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 139:23–24 (id: 24075)

As the psalmist prays about the wicked, he also reflects on himself. He does not assume that he is righteous. This prayer has been spoken again and again by God’s people over many generations.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 139:24 (id: 24076)

The path of wisdom leads to eternal life (see [Psalm 1:6](https://ref.ly/Ps1:6); [Proverbs 4:18](https://ref.ly/Prov4:18); [6:23](https://ref.ly/Prov6:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 139:24


## Psalms 140:1–3 (id: 24091)

The psalmist prays for the Lord to rescue him from wicked people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Psalms 140:1–13 (id: 24090)

In this personal lament, the psalmist describes God's enemies and their evil plans. He prays for the Lord's protection and rescue, trusting in God's strength and justice. He predicts that fire will judge the evil ([Psalm 140:9–11](https://ref.ly/Ps140:9-Ps140:11)) and that he will be proven right and live in the Lord's presence ([140:13](https://ref.ly/Ps140:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:1-13

## Psalms 140:3 (id: 24093)

*snakes … vipers*: These creatures portray the wicked as very corrupt people (see [Proverbs 23:32](https://ref.ly/Prov23:32); [Isaiah 14:29](https://ref.ly/Isa14:29); [Romans 3:13](https://ref.ly/Rom3:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Psalms 140:4–5 (id: 24094)

The psalmist prays for God's protection (see [Psalm 121](https://ref.ly/Ps121:1-Ps121:8)), asking the Lord to keep him safe from the wicked's many plans.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`)

## Psalms 140:7 (id: 24095)

*O GOD the Lord*: This title comes from combining the name *Yahweh* with the title *Adonai* (which means Master). See also [Psalms 68:20](https://ref.ly/Ps68:20); [69:6](https://ref.ly/Ps69:6); [71:16](https://ref.ly/Ps71:16); [73:28](https://ref.ly/Ps73:28); [109:21](https://ref.ly/Ps109:21); [141:8](https://ref.ly/Ps141:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 140:9–11 (id: 24096)

The psalmist prays for God’s judgment on the wicked.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:9-11


## Psalms 140:10 (id: 24092)

The psalmist asks for *burning coals* to fall on his enemies and for them to be thrown *into the miry pits*, so that they cannot attack again. This language hints at the New Testament teachings on hell (see [Matthew 5:22](https://ref.ly/Matt5:22), [29–30](https://ref.ly/Matt5:29-Matt5:30); [10:28](https://ref.ly/Matt10:28); [18:9](https://ref.ly/Matt18:9); [Mark 9:43–47](https://ref.ly/Mark9:43-Mark9:47); [Luke 12:5](https://ref.ly/Luke12:5); [James 3:6](https://ref.ly/Jas3:6); [2 Peter 2:4](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 140:10


## Psalms 141:1–2 (id: 24098)

* The psalmist urgently prays for rescue. He does not have incense or an evening offering ([Exodus 29:38–39](https://ref.ly/Exod29:38-Exod29:39)), but his prayers and devotion represent those elements (see [Isaiah 1:13](https://ref.ly/Isa1:13); [Revelation 5:8](https://ref.ly/Rev5:8)).
* *uplifted hands*: This is a posture of prayer ([Psalms 28:2](https://ref.ly/Ps28:2); [88:9](https://ref.ly/Ps88:9); [143:6](https://ref.ly/Ps143:6); [Exodus 9:29](https://ref.ly/Exod9:29); [1 Timothy 2:8](https://ref.ly/1Tim2:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 141:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 141:1–10 (id: 24097)

The psalmist prays for rescue and for wisdom. He also looks ahead to the end of evil. The idea that people receive what they deserve ([Psalm 141:10](https://ref.ly/Ps141:10)) holds the psalm together.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 141:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); David (ID: `person:David`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Net (ID: `realia:Net`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 141:3–5 (id: 24099)

* Wisdom sometimes requires protection from oneself. People might say or think things that lead to a harmful path ([Proverbs 13:3](https://ref.ly/Prov13:3); [21:23](https://ref.ly/Prov21:23)).
* Wisdom accepts correction from godly people (see [Proverbs 3:11](https://ref.ly/Prov3:11); [9:8](https://ref.ly/Prov9:8); [15:31](https://ref.ly/Prov15:31); [19:25](https://ref.ly/Prov19:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 141:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`)

## Psalms 142:1–3 (id: 24100)

Although overwhelmed by his persecutors ([Psalm 142:6](https://ref.ly/Ps142:6)), the psalmist seeks wisdom from the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Beg for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:BegForMercy`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 142:1–7 (id: 24101)

*when he was in the cave*: Compare [Psalm 57:1](https://ref.ly/Ps57:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Beg for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:BegForMercy`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 142:7 (id: 24105)

He feels trapped and stressed, similar to being in prison (see [Psalm 107:10](https://ref.ly/Ps107:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Beg for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:BegForMercy`); Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 142:3–4 (id: 24102)

The psalmist feels deep despair (see [Psalm 77:3](https://ref.ly/Ps77:3)), and the Lord helps him in his distress. The psalmist trusts in the Lord's wisdom (see [Psalm 139](https://ref.ly/Ps139:1-Ps139:24)) but mourns being trapped by evil people ([5:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ps5:9-Ps5:10); [140:5](https://ref.ly/Ps140:5); [141:9](https://ref.ly/Ps141:9); [142:3](https://ref.ly/Ps142:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Psalms 142:5 (id: 24103)

Even when alone, the psalmist trusts the Lord, who is his life (see [Psalms 9:9](https://ref.ly/Ps9:9); [16:5](https://ref.ly/Ps16:5); [27:13](https://ref.ly/Ps27:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 142:6–7 (id: 24104)

Though overwhelmed by loneliness and trouble, the psalmist focuses on the Lord’s goodness and protection. He knows that the Lord alone is his Redeemer, so he offers his praise.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:6-7


## Psalms 142 (id: 24106)

The psalmist faces severe persecution, so he cries to the Lord, and the Lord will rescue him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 142:1-7


## Psalms 143:1–2 (id: 24108)

The psalmist prays for God’s love and mercy.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); David (ID: `person:David`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Psalms 143:1–12 (id: 24107)

In this lament, the psalmist feels overwhelmed by constant trouble from his enemies, so he turns to the Lord’s love, righteousness, and faithfulness. He remembers what God has done in the past and longs for God to renew his love. He opens himself to God’s wisdom because he knows that God’s instruction leads to life.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); Psalm (ID: `keyterm:Psalm`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); David (ID: `person:David`); Plea for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:Supplication`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Psalms 143:2 (id: 24111)

Everyone has sinned and is guilty before God ([Romans 3:20–24](https://ref.ly/Rom3:20-Rom3:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Psalms 143:3–4 (id: 24112)

*My spirit grows faint within me*: The psalmist admits his desperation as his life seems to slip away under cruel and powerful enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:3-4


## Psalms 143:5–6 (id: 24113)

Remembering God’s acts from the past reassures the psalmist of God’s faithfulness ([Psalms 42:4](https://ref.ly/Ps42:4); [63:6](https://ref.ly/Ps63:6); [77:3](https://ref.ly/Ps77:3), [6](https://ref.ly/Ps77:6); [119:55](https://ref.ly/Ps119:55)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:5-6


## Psalms 143:6 (id: 24114)

The psalmist’s thirst is a deep longing for God’s redemption (see [Psalm 42:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps42:1-Ps42:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:6


## Psalms 143:7 (id: 24116)

The psalmist experiences depression. He describes himself as close to death from sorrow ([Psalms 31:10](https://ref.ly/Ps31:10); [119:87](https://ref.ly/Ps119:87)). He feels worn out ([Psalms 39:10](https://ref.ly/Ps39:10); [71:9](https://ref.ly/Ps71:9); [119:81](https://ref.ly/Ps119:81)). He is weak from longing ([Psalm 84:2](https://ref.ly/Ps84:2)). He feels overwhelmed by the burdens of life ([Psalm 90:7](https://ref.ly/Ps90:7), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps90:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 143:7–10 (id: 24115)

The psalmist asks the Lord to renew his love.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:7-10

* The psalmist prays for wisdom (see [Psalms 5:8](https://ref.ly/Ps5:8); [25:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps25:4-Ps25:6); [138](https://ref.ly/Ps138:1-Ps138:8); [143:10](https://ref.ly/Ps143:10)).
* *Your good Spirit*: The Spirit brings God's goodness (see [Psalm 23:6](https://ref.ly/Ps23:6)).

*revive*: Here, it means to bring someone out of despair and restore them to life ([Psalms 80:19](https://ref.ly/Ps80:19); [85:6](https://ref.ly/Ps85:6); [138:7](https://ref.ly/Ps138:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:11

## Psalms 143:12 (id: 24110)

The psalm ends with a prayer for God to show that the psalmist is in the right and to restore his life.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 143:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 144:1–2 (id: 24119)

*my Rock … my steadfast love … my deliverer … my shield*: The psalmist’s descriptive names for the Lord reveal his deep love for him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Psalms 144:1–15 (id: 24118)

This lament begins with an exclamation of love for the Lord. The community then reflects on the temporary nature of life ([Psalm 144:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps144:3-Ps144:4)). The psalmist asks for God's help and anticipates victory ([Psalm 144:5–11](https://ref.ly/Ps144:5-Ps144:11)). 

A new song ([Psalm 144:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ps144:9-Ps144:10)) and a prayer for rescue ([144:11](https://ref.ly/Ps144:11)) introduce the theme of the Lord’s blessings through his care and protection ([Psalm 144:12–15](https://ref.ly/Ps144:12-Ps144:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:1-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); David (ID: `person:David`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Shield (ID: `keyterm:Shield`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`); Small Shield (ID: `realia:SmallShield`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 144:3–4 (id: 24123)

*what is man*: The same question in [Psalm 8:4–6](https://ref.ly/Ps8:4-Ps8:6) leads to the answer that humans are only mortal (they do not live forever), yet God gives them honor and responsibility to rule. 

The psalmist focuses on how brief and fragile human life is. He describes it as temporary and passing quickly (see [Psalms 90:3](https://ref.ly/Ps90:3), [7–10](https://ref.ly/Ps90:7-Ps90:10); [102:11](https://ref.ly/Ps102:11); [109:23](https://ref.ly/Ps109:23); [146:4](https://ref.ly/Ps146:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 144:5–8 (id: 24124)

The psalmist asks God to act against his enemies (compare [Psalm 18:7–19](https://ref.ly/Ps18:7-Ps18:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:5-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 144:12 (id: 24121)

The psalmist compares sons and daughters to plants and pillars, which are images of strength and beauty.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:12


## Psalms 144:12–15 (id: 24120)

This prayer for the Lord’s blessing brings the laments of [Psalms 140–144](https://ref.ly/Ps140:1-Ps144:15) to a close. It describes God’s provision (care for needs) and protection (keeping people safe; see [Psalm 127](https://ref.ly/Ps127:1-Ps127:5)), not only for the psalmist but also for his children and for all who follow God.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:12-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 144:13–14 (id: 24122)

*no going into captivity, and no cry of lament*: These images show peace and safety.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 144:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Psalms 145:1–3 (id: 24126)

The psalmist praises God, the Great King who reigns over all other rulers (see [Psalm 47:2](https://ref.ly/Ps47:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 145:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 145:1–21 (id: 24125)

This psalm of praise is the last in this collection of the psalms of David ([Psalms 138–145](https://ref.ly/Ps138:1-Ps145:21)). Praise continues in the last five psalms ([Psalms 146–150](https://ref.ly/Ps146:1-Ps150:6)). 

This psalm celebrates the Lord as the King of all ([Psalm 145:1–3](https://ref.ly/Ps145:1-Ps145:3), [10–13](https://ref.ly/Ps145:10-Ps145:13)). God's actions show his royal qualities ([Psalm 145:4–7](https://ref.ly/Ps145:4-Ps145:7)). He cares for all creatures ([Psalm 145:14–17](https://ref.ly/Ps145:14-Ps145:17)) and for those who fear him ([145:18–20](https://ref.ly/Ps145:18-Ps145:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 145:1-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); Loyal Person (ID: `keyterm:LoyalPerson`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); David (ID: `person:David`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`)

## Psalms 145:4–7 (id: 24130)

This litany (a repeated form of praise, often with the same line said again and again) celebrates God’s character and his mighty deeds. Each generation will continue to tell others about God’s power ([Psalm 22:30](https://ref.ly/Ps22:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 145:4-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Psalms 145:20 (id: 24129)

The Lord cares for those who love him and fear him. God\-fearers love the Lord and find refuge in him ([Psalm 5:11](https://ref.ly/Ps5:11)). They seek him as their only true Savior ([Psalms 40:16](https://ref.ly/Ps40:16); [70:4](https://ref.ly/Ps70:4)). They will experience a good future ([Psalm 69:36](https://ref.ly/Ps69:36)) and enjoy the peace that God gives ([119:165](https://ref.ly/Ps119:165)).

Human achievements do not impress God, who commands nature. Instead, he finds joy in those who fear him.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:7-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Praise (ID: `keyterm:Praise`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 147:8 (id: 24146)

God has complete authority and power over the weather ([Psalms 77:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps77:17-Ps77:18); [78:26](https://ref.ly/Ps78:26); [104:4](https://ref.ly/Ps104:4); [148:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps148:5-Ps148:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Psalms 147:10–11 (id: 24137)

*the legs of a man*: Any physical or mental strength comes from the Lord, like the strength of a horse. These gifts are reasons to praise the Lord, not to boast ([Jeremiah 9:23–24](https://ref.ly/Jer9:23-Jer9:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`)

## Psalms 147:13 (id: 24138)

* *the bars of your gates*: These represent protection from enemies.
* God blesses his people by giving them safety, prosperity, peace, and the things they need (see [Psalm 133:3](https://ref.ly/Ps133:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Psalms 147:15–18 (id: 24139)

Simply by speaking his command*,* the Lord can change the season from harsh winter to refreshing spring (see [Psalms 147:8](https://ref.ly/Ps147:8); [148:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps148:5-Ps148:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:15-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Wool (ID: `realia:Wool`)

## Psalms 147:19–20 (id: 24140)

*word … statutes … judgments*: The Lord gave powerful words to Israel so that they might obey him ([Psalm 105:45](https://ref.ly/Ps105:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 147:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`)

## Psalms 148:1 (id: 24150)

When Jesus entered Jerusalem, the crowds welcomed him like a king and shouted blessings, including a phrase from this verse ([Matthew 21:9](https://ref.ly/Matt21:9); [Mark 11:10](https://ref.ly/Mark11:10); [Luke 19:38](https://ref.ly/Luke19:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 148:1–14 (id: 24147)

This psalm calls for the whole created order to praise the Lord because he has honored his faithful people.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:1-14

## Psalms 148:6 (id: 24151)

The created order is stable because of the Lord's word (see [Psalm 33:9](https://ref.ly/Ps33:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`)

## Psalms 148:8 (id: 24152)

In the ancient Near East, people believed that different gods controlled different parts of nature. But the Lord rules over all of nature. Every part of creation serves him and brings him praise by obeying him ([Psalms 77:17–18](https://ref.ly/Ps77:17-Ps77:18); [104:4](https://ref.ly/Ps104:4); [135:7](https://ref.ly/Ps135:7); [147:8](https://ref.ly/Ps147:8), [15–18](https://ref.ly/Ps147:15-Ps147:18); [Numbers 11:31](https://ref.ly/Num11:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:8


## Psalms 148:11–12 (id: 24148)

The psalmist calls all humans to offer praise to God, regardless of status, age, or gender.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:11-12


## Psalms 148:13–14 (id: 24149)

* Praise of the Lord by all people is right because he is glorious and has blessed his people (see [Psalm 147:13](https://ref.ly/Ps147:13); [Isaiah 46:13](https://ref.ly/Isa46:13); [Ephesians 3:10](https://ref.ly/Eph3:10)).
* *His saint* are God's trusting and devoted servants ([Psalm 86:2](https://ref.ly/Ps86:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 148:13-14

## Psalms 149:1–9 (id: 24153)

This psalm of praise celebrates the Lord's victory. God is the Maker of Israel ([Psalm 149:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ps149:1-Ps149:2)) and the victorious King who shares his glory with his faithful followers ([149:3–9](https://ref.ly/Ps149:3-Ps149:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 149:1-9

## Psalms 149:4 (id: 24154)

God’s people, whom the world ignores and looks down on, will be shown to be right ([Matthew 19:30](https://ref.ly/Matt19:30)). The humble will overcome those who oppress and persecute them (see [Isaiah 61:3](https://ref.ly/Isa61:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 149:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 149:6–9 (id: 24155)

*vengeance*: The Lord is the one who brings justice against wrongdoing (see [Psalms 2:3](https://ref.ly/Ps2:3); [94:1](https://ref.ly/Ps94:1); [102:20](https://ref.ly/Ps102:20); [142:7](https://ref.ly/Ps142:7); [146:7](https://ref.ly/Ps146:7)).

## Psalms 150:1 (id: 24162)

The phrase *mighty heavens* refers to [Genesis 1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31) and God's amazing work in filling the emptiness.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 150:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Psalms 150:1–6 (id: 24161)

Everything that breathes is commanded to praise the Lord as he deserves. The whole Psalter describes the greatness of the Lord, so this psalm is a fitting conclusion.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 150:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Round Dance (ID: `keyterm:RoundDance`); Cymbals (ID: `realia:Cymbals`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`)

## Psalms 150:3–5 (id: 24163)

* The psalmist calls all people to praise God with instruments and dancing.
* *flutes*: These are wind instruments (instruments you blow into to make sound). The Bible does not mention them as part of formal temple worship. This suggests they were used more in everyday life or celebrations, rather than in official worship at the temple.
* *clashing cymbals*: Temple musicians clapped two bronze bowls together to make a loud sound as part of the music.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 150:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Round Dance (ID: `keyterm:RoundDance`); Cymbals (ID: `realia:Cymbals`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`)

## Psalms 150:6 (id: 24164)

*praise the LORD!*: This refrain is a fitting conclusion to the entire book of Psalms.

* **Associated Passages:** Psalms 150:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
